A bidi control right after a run in the same text is kept in the run's segment, which then doesn't end with the run, so the Gecko profile gives no hang to a text's run before one, nor to an item's last run that only bidi controls follow to the paragraph's end: `東京都`, U+3000 and U+200E in 16px Arial with Hiragino Sans are one line at 48-63px in Firefox, as one text node and as two items, and `東京` / `都` with the run in the profile, and `大阪市`, U+3000, U+200E and `次` in one text node are `大阪市` with the run / `次` at those widths in Firefox and `大阪` / `市`, the run and `次` in the profile. The Blink profile keeps the control in the segment too, and has Chrome's lines for those two text nodes except under 32px, where a line holds one ideograph and Chrome hangs the run on it (Rich-inline item edges, the sweep after #460; Chrome 154.0.8037.98 and Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-10). The Gecko scan also gives no break after a U+3000 that follows a Hebrew letter, where Firefox ends the line after it, hanging: `שלום`, U+3000 and `עולם טוב` in a right-to-left paragraph are `שלום` / `עולם` / `טוב` at 33-45px in Firefox, in one text node and as two items, where the profile starts the second line with the U+3000 and cuts the word after it; after Latin, Arabic and Han letters the scan gives the break (Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-10, and for the other letters a stand-in Canvas; not traced in Firefox's source).
- At a soft-hyphen break right after U+3000, Chrome paints no hyphen and Pretext adds `-` and overflows (`a`, U+3000, U+00AD, `b` in pre-wrap), and Chrome never lets a soft hyphen at a line start hold a line its hyphen doesn't fit (39 cases; 6 / 0 / 2; 2026-09-24). In rich inline, where an item ends with the run and the next starts with or is the soft hyphen, the hyphen's room sends the line back a break: 40 Chrome layouts of `文字`, U+3000 and `\u00ADabc` or U+00AD, `ab` at 16-160px that main passes, by an item's own hang and no hyphen charged at an item's end (2026-10-05).
- Chrome and Firefox shape a word whole across its soft hyphens, where Pretext measures the text on each side of one apart. That is WebKit's layout, whose text items end at a soft hyphen and are measured alone (`InlineItemsBuilder.cpp:1015-1034`; `TextUtil::width`, `TextUtil.cpp:62-100`; WebKit 7625.1.29.11.27). Blink keeps the soft hyphen in the text it shapes (`ShouldIgnore`, `inline_items_builder.cc:164-173`, Chromium 153) and HarfBuzz's lookups step over a default ignorable (`may_skip`, `hb-ot-layout-gsubgpos.hh:558-571`; the legacy `kern` table by the same iterator, `hb-kern.hh:56-75`), so Chrome kerns across a ZWSP too; Gecko drops the soft hyphen before it shapes (`IsDiscardable`, `nsTextFrameUtils.cpp:32-49`) and ends a shaped word at a ZWSP or a word joiner (`IsInvalidChar`, `gfxTextRun.h:975-992`), so Firefox kerns across a soft hyphen and not across a ZWSP. In both, the letters kern across the soft hyphen, and ligate in Firefox and in some fonts in Chrome (`of\u00ADfice` in Roboto is 0.76px narrower), and a line that holds both sides is as wide as the word without the soft hyphen, wider or narrower than its syllables' sum. In 16px Helvetica Neue `ter\u00ADna` is 36.74px wide in Chrome and 36.73px in Firefox, as `terna` is, where its syllables measure 36.45px and 36.47px; webkit-host lays it out 36.45px wide. Up to 36.7px both browsers break it at the soft hyphen where Pretext reports one line, but the word without the soft hyphen misses there too, by the grapheme sums of Emergency breaks, so prose sizes the gap. Of 12 hyphenated words in 22 fonts at 16px, a word on one line is 0.05px or more wider than predicted in 38 of the 264 pairs in Chrome and 37 in Firefox, up to 0.81px (`o\u00ADver\u00ADview` in Hoefler Text), and narrower in 56 and 60, up to 0.88px; pairs of capitals differ by up to 3px (`Y\u00ADo` in Hoefler Text), and only Georgia and Menlo differ on no string. Of 1,708 layouts of two hand-hyphenated paragraphs, the Gatsby opening and a German one, in 14 fonts at 120-360px, Chrome fails 62, 5 of them with a wrong line count, and Firefox 54, 6 with a wrong line count, where the paragraphs without their soft hyphens fail 48 and 4; webkit-host fails none with them and 2 without. The Gatsby paragraph in 16px Verdana at 340px is 15 lines in Chrome and Firefox and 16 in Pretext. In the layouts that pass, 5,359 of 28,746 lines in Chrome and 5,679 of 28,895 in Firefox are reported more than 0.05px off the browser's, and none in webkit-host (Chrome 154.0.8037.57, Firefox 156.0.1, webkit-host, 2026-10-04). Where a line breaks at the soft hyphen, Chrome shapes the line's end again (`ShapingLineBreaker::ShapeLine`, `shaping_line_breaker.cc:519-553`) and adds the hyphen as a string shaped apart (`LineBreaker::AddHyphen`, `line_breaker.cc:732-749`), so neither side keeps the kerning and the hyphen line is as predicted, in all 154 lines of a probe. Firefox keeps the shaped word's advances and adds the hyphen's width (`BreakAndMeasureText`, `gfxTextRun.cpp:1085-1089`, `:1139-1150`), so the syllable before the hyphen keeps the whole adjustment where HarfBuzz put it all on the first glyph, as GPOS does, and half in a font whose pairs are in the legacy `kern` table (`hb-kern.hh:102-106`): in 16px Gill Sans `T-` before `oooo` is 12.43px wide in Firefox and 14.83px in the Gecko profile, and 117 of the 154 hyphen lines are off (which table a font's pairs are in is inferred from that share, not read from the fonts). A line's text leaves out its unbroken soft hyphens (`src/line-text.ts`), which in Chrome and Firefox paints the page's own width, off from the reported one as above: of 1,863 and 1,877 prose lines that hold a soft hyphen and don't end at one, 108 and 104 paint more than 0.05px wider than reported, at most 0.97px, and 253 and 259 narrower. In Safari the page with its soft hyphens matches the report and the painted text doesn't: 145 of 1,874 such lines paint wider, at most 1.73px, and 244 narrower, against 41 and 2 with the soft hyphens kept, which are the probe's own, as it painted a line without its trailing space (webkit-host, 2026-10-04); whether a line's text keeps its soft hyphens is a question for the API discussion. A port starts from the measurement both profiles already make: each soft hyphen's two neighbours joined (`getJoinedNarrowing()`, `src/prepare.ts`), kept only where it narrows and read only where a hyphen overflows (below). The Gecko profile's joined string, the syllables concatenated, measures the page's width in 440 of 440 word and font pairs; the Chromium profile's needs U+2060 between the syllables (440 of 440), since concatenated they ligate where the page doesn't in 10 of the 440 (`fi` and `ff` in Helvetica Neue, Hoefler Text, Futura, Didot, Baskerville, Palatino and Gill Sans). A prototype that isn't checked in took that difference signed, added it to the later syllable's width, took it back where that syllable starts a line, and left the first glyph's share with the hyphen line in the Gecko profile: the prose failures went from 62 to 14 in Chrome, all 14 lines that end in a comma or full stop in Gill Sans and PT Sans (Kerning with spaces), and from 54 to 2 in Firefox, with 1 Firefox layout lost, and of the recorded cases that hold a soft hyphen it fixed 8 and lost 4 of Chrome's 3,319 and fixed 29 and lost none of Firefox's 3,061. What it leaves: Canvas can't say which glyph holds a pair's adjustment, so the Gecko profile's hyphen line takes a premise, and all of it on the first glyph is half the kerning off in the `kern`-table fonts (65 of the 154 hyphen lines); a joined difference that isn't kerning, as Arabic letters, emoji sequences and marks across the soft hyphen, where its 4 lost Chrome cases are (2 in Noto Naskh Arabic at 1px letter spacing and 2 of a skin tone after a soft hyphen, not traced); and rich items whose edge is at the soft hyphen, a second syllable that takes an emergency break, and letter spacing, which it didn't build. It wasn't benchmarked or run through the gate, and its numbers have had no second check (RESEARCH.md, Evaluation Traps). The harness holds little of this: the sample's soft-hyphenated draws are stand-ins, long words with a soft hyphen every few letters, and the one case under "Real usage: soft hyphens" on Chrome's list, in Roboto, where Chrome moves the syllable before the hyphen to the next line, is this kerning (with the syllables measured joined its lines are Chrome's, 647.55px and 224.05px wide). The other case listed there until #446, in Hiragino Sans, was Chrome's return to a break between two ideographs (below). Catalog cases would be a pair that widens and one that narrows across a soft hyphen, in a GPOS font and a `kern`-table one, either side of the width where the word fits. WebKit's 15 such real-usage cases were its return from an unfit hyphen (#396).
- Chrome and Safari keep a soft hyphen at a paragraph or hard-break start, where Firefox and Pretext consume it. Still failing after #349, on the stand-in Canvas at b1fd05fc: soft hyphens ending the text after a line feed ("Soft hyphens alone after the last line feed", 8 / 0 / 5), a paragraph of only soft hyphens, and one starting a chunk before a word that doesn't fit. The likeliest fix is one line-start rule for soft hyphens and ZWSP (by 2026-09-12). Such a rule has two more facts to take (2026-09-27). Chrome and Safari keep a collapsible space that follows a soft hyphen where a line starts, and it takes room there: items `café`, ` \u00AD \u00AD`, `.`, `ab` in 16px Arial at 37.3px start their second line with the space after the first soft hyphen, 26.69px wide, in both, as their text in one node does in Chrome, where the plain-text walker consumes both (16 Chrome and 21 webkit-host cases of a 28,435-case rich probe, RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries). And all three browsers keep a ZWSP where a line starts after a break at a space, after a soft hyphen too, so it holds a line where the word after it doesn't fit: `ab `, ZWSP, `xyzwxyz` and `ab `, U+00AD, ZWSP, `xyzwxyz` at 20px in 16px Arial take an empty second line in Chrome, Firefox and Safari, in one text node and as spans, where the plain-text walker consumes the ZWSP, and rich inline with it since #460; the walk item by item kept a ZWSP that starts an item (Rich-inline item edges, the sweep after #460). A rich line's first fragment starts after what its start consumes, as a flat line does, since 2026-09-29; the rich set's items ` ­ ­​`, `textword` passed in 8 Chrome and 6 webkit-host cases before only because the fragment started before the consumed space Chrome and Safari show.
- In Chrome, a soft hyphen right after collapsible white space ends no line where it starts past the line's width, since Blink finds the offset at the width before it, at the white space, and breaks there (`ShapingLineBreaker::ShapeLine`, `shaping_line_breaker.cc`); and where Blink breaks at white space right after a soft hyphen, the break takes that hyphen, since once the white space hangs, the break's offset follows a soft hyphen (`SetBreakOffset`). In 16px Arial `ab \u00ADcd ef` gives `ab` / `cd` with no hyphen at 20px, and `ab \u00AD cd` paints `ab -` at 41px. The Chromium profile takes neither rule: it ends the first line after the soft hyphen with the hyphen overflowing, which the harness's pass rule doesn't see for one text node, and ends the second at the white space without the hyphen. Where its hyphen doesn't fit a narrow line, Chrome also breaks inside the word, as for items `of`, `\u00ADfic`, `e` at 18px. Rich inline shows the first rule where the soft hyphen is in an item that continues a run from an earlier item: the line returns from the unfit hyphen to the break before the run, where Chrome ends it at the white space, as for items `–xyz漢a`, `b  \u00AD\u00ADab` at 67px: most of the 62 Chrome and 2 webkit-host cases of a 43,462-case rich probe that main passed and that hold a soft hyphen after white space inside an item. The first rule alone, in plain text and rich inline, fixed 98 Chrome cases of another probe and lost 32, which the second rule decides, so the two land together, as a plain-text change (2026-09-27, Chrome 154).
- Safari moves a soft hyphen right after collapsible white space to the next line where that white space ends past the line's width, and Pretext hangs the white space and keeps the soft hyphen on the line. Before a hard break or the paragraph's end the soft hyphen then has a line of its own: `shy after space`, a space and U+00AD in 16px Inter at 120px is 2 lines in webkit-host and 1 in Pretext, and 3 against 2 with U+2028 and `after` behind it. With `after` right behind it the lines agree, and Pretext's first one carries a hyphen Safari's doesn't (128.16px against 116.3px). Not traced (webkit-host, 2026-10-01).
- In Safari a line that ends at U+2028 or U+2029 right after a collapsible space is as wide as its text, and Pretext's is a space wider: WebKit removes a line's trailing trimmable content where the line closes (`InlineLineBuilder.cpp:646`), and a line break appended after a collapsible space leaves it trimmable (`Line::appendLineBreak`, `InlineLine.cpp:588-597`), where the walker counts the space of a chunk that ends at a hard break. `xx`, two spaces, U+2028, two spaces, `yy` in 16px Arial is 16px wide on its first line in webkit-host and 20.45px in Pretext. No line moves; a line's `width`, `maxLineWidth` and `measureNaturalWidth()` do. A word joiner or ZWSP between the space and the separator keeps the space in both. None of the sample's 11,901 draws holds either character. A fix in `walkPreparedComplexLines`, 7 lines that paint the width of the break after that space, moved 799 line widths of 1,195 such inputs in webkit-host, each to Safari's, and read 4.3% slower on Safari's pre-wrap layout row in both of two bench sessions (2026-10-01). The fix is commit 9864e15f, which #417 reverts; it waits on text that needs it or a place that costs nothing.
- Chromium keeps no-break characters on a soft hyphen's line, where WebKit breaks before them; trace Blink first (by 2026-09-12).
- Where a soft hyphen's hyphen doesn't fit, the Chromium profile returns since #446 to a break between two text segments too, as after `-`, a dash or `?` and between ideographs, where its line leaves room for the hyphen, as Blink's second pass at the width less the hyphen finds it (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Chrome, under soft hyphens, has the rule and the probe's 34,064 layouts; Chrome 154.0.8037.57, 2026-10-05). Before, it kept the hyphen, up to its whole width past the line's end, wherever such a break lay after the latest space, ZWSP or soft hyphen that left room (#433). What is left, each a hyphen line the profile reports past the width where Chrome fits it, so that it returns and Chrome doesn't, in layouts that agreed before only because the hyphen was kept:
  - Kerning across the break. The line is summed from segments measured apart, and Chrome shapes it whole: in 16px Inter `Bitte die Nebenrollen-Ta-` is 187.88px wide in Chrome and 189.06px summed, the 1.18px being the kerning between `-` and `T`, so at 188px and 189px Chrome keeps the hyphen and the profile ends the line after `Nebenrollen-` (4 of 1,683 probe layouts after a hyphen-minus, two of them the same line as a paragraph's second, and none in Arial, Georgia, Helvetica Neue or Times New Roman). Firefox keeps the hyphen there too, and the Gecko profile, which returned to such breaks before, fails those 4 on main. Between kana it is the kerning Chrome's Canvas doesn't report (Kerning Chrome's Canvas doesn't report): `日本語の文章を借\u00ADりるときは注意\u00ADしてくだ\u00ADさい。` in 16px Hiragino Sans keeps `…してくだ-`, 308.3px wide, at 308.5-309.5px in Chrome, where the profile sums 309.73px (3 of 581 layouts at 40-330px).
  - A padded span's end edge. Rich inline fits a hyphen inside a padded span with the span's end edge, which Chrome doesn't count on a line that ends inside the span (Rich-inline item edges), so for the width of the end padding Chrome keeps the hyphen and the profile returns: a 13px box and #433's German sentence in a span with 2.5px of padding in 10px Georgia keep `Nebenrollen-Ta-` on the first line at 129-131px in Chrome, in 4 lines, where the profile has 5 (22 of 570 probe layouts of that box and span, 8 with a wrong line count, against 29 fixed; at the 128px the issue gives, the profile now has Chrome's 5 lines). Each of the 8 has one line more than Chrome, so a box sized from it is a line too tall and its text doesn't overflow: in the 570 layouts the profile had fewer lines than Chrome in 9 before and in none after, and more lines in 20 before and in 36 after. The rich set holds the shape, a 13px box and a span of `Bitte die Nebenrollen-Ta\u00ADkes vor` with 2.5px of padding in 10px Georgia: 1/64px above the 128.54px where Chrome's hyphen fits, Chrome keeps the hyphen and the profile returns (accepted), and 1/64px below it both return.
  - A break right after a fullwidth closing mark is fit with the mark at its full width, where Blink's second pass can halve a mark the reduced width falls inside (`shaping_line_breaker.cc:342-363`, Chromium 153). That needs a syllable after the mark narrower than the hyphen; `日本「文章」借\u00ADりる日本「文」章\u00AD借りる` in 16px Hiragino Sans passes at all 581 widths, and no failing layout is known.
  - An item edge between the break and the soft hyphen, in one font. Rich inline returns across items as in one text (Rich-inline item edges, Returns from an unfit hyphen), and what fails there is kerning across the edge (Rich-inline item edges, Measured apart): Chrome shapes two same-font items as one run and kerns `T` with `a`, so of `Bitte die Nebenrollen-T` and a span of `a\u00ADkes vor` in 16px Arial the line `Nebenrollen-Ta-` is 113.84px wide in Chrome and 115.61px summed from items measured apart. 18 of 277 probe layouts at 100-238px fail, on main alike, and none of 277 with the span in bold (Chrome 154.0.8037.98, 2026-10-06).
  - Chrome gives no break after `!` or a slash before a letter, so there the soft hyphen is the line's first opportunity and Chrome breaks the word between letters, as under "A hyphen wider than the syllable after it" below (17 of 414 probe layouts after `!` and 57 of 1,683 after a slash, as on main).
- Chrome charges a hyphen for a soft hyphen that ends the text or stands before a hard break, and returns from it where that hyphen doesn't fit; the Chromium profile gives such a soft hyphen no hyphen and no return. Blink marks a break at a text item's end hyphenated where a soft hyphen comes right before it (`SetBreakOffset`, `shaping_line_breaker.cc:211-225`, called with the range's end at `:295` and `:369`, Chromium 153), so `BreakText`'s retry at the width less the hyphen runs there as at any other soft hyphen (`line_breaker.cc:1705-1718`). `Bitte die Nebenrollen-Ta\u00AD` in 16px Arial at 109-113px is `Bitte die`, `Nebenrollen-`, `Ta` in Chrome and 2 lines in the profile, whose second, `Nebenrollen-Ta`, is 108.51px wide, and the same text before a line feed in pre-wrap does the same: 46 of 552 probe layouts of the two shapes, each with a wrong line count, in 16px Arial and Georgia (Chrome 154.0.8037.57, 2026-10-05). Chrome's last line is as wide as `Ta` alone, so it paints no hyphen there. The walker makes a soft hyphen a pending break only where a segment other than a hard break follows it (`walkPreparedComplexLines`, `src/line-break.ts`). What Firefox and Safari do there wasn't checked.
- At a chosen soft hyphen the engines paint U+2010 where a font has a glyph for it, else `-`: WebKit and Blink where the primary font has it (`hyphenString`, `ComputedStyle::HyphenString`), Gecko where any listed font or its default font does (`MakeHyphenTextRun` over `GetFirstValidFont(U+2010)`, `gfxTextRun.cpp:2458-2473` and `2277-2360`). Since #396 each profile measures its engine's choice (`hyphenFromPrimaryFont`, `getHyphenText()`), where all measured `-`: `foo trans\u00ADatlantic` in 16px Inter, whose U+2010 is 5.73px and whose `-` 7.36px, keeps `foo trans-` on one line from 73.02px in all three browsers, and did from 74.64px in the profiles. Rich inline measures the same hyphen, also where an item's only soft hyphen starts it, as in items `foo trans` and `\u00ADatlantic`: the Gecko scan gives no break after a soft hyphen that starts a text, so the item holds it as zero-width glue, and the text the items join ends a line there. On 5,700 fresh cases of soft-hyphenated text (462 font strings, prose in 12 languages, 24 text shapes, rich items, letter spacing; 24-600px) the Chromium profile gained 44 and lost 4 and the Gecko profile gained 43 and lost 1, and the font strings with a hyphen line more than 0.05px off went from 111 to 28 in Chrome and from 147 to 73 in Firefox, none newly off (Chrome 154.0.8037.57, Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-01; webkit-host's went from 216 to 0). What is left:
  - The primary-font check (`getHyphenText()`, the WebKit and Chromium profiles) takes the premise that the primary font draws a space. The engines' primary font is the first listed family's that gives a font, with a space glyph or not (`primaryFont`, `FontCascadeFonts.h:225-254`), and Canvas can't tell a family without a font from one without the character. So a first family whose font has no space, an icon font, is skipped where the engines take it. A family split into faces by `unicode-range` is asked whole, where the engines ask only the face whose range holds the space (`FontCascadeFonts.h:230-234`, `FontRanges.cpp:83-118`): where the space and U+2010 sit in different faces, as they can in a sliced Chinese or Japanese web font, the browser paints `-` and the profile measures U+2010, which such a font can draw a full em wide. The Latin slice Google Fonts serves holds both, so its Latin families aren't affected. And where no listed family gives a font and the list names no generic family, the check answers `-`, where the engines ask their last-resort font. None of the three is sized.
  - The check also needs Canvas's `monospace` and `serif` to measure a space and U+2010 differently, as they do on macOS; where they don't, the profile measures `-`, as before #396. iOS Safari, Android and Windows aren't checked.
  - The Gecko profile measures U+2010 as Canvas draws it, on the premise that Firefox's default font has one, as macOS's user font, Helvetica, does (`GetDefaultFontForPlatform`, `CoreTextFontList.cpp:1978-1989`); 16px Geeza Pro, Damascus and Symbol, which have none, paint a fallback font's under every page language tried. Where neither a listed family nor the default font has one, Firefox paints `-`. Windows, Linux and Android aren't checked.
  - The Chromium and Gecko profiles keep a hyphen that overflows by less than the line's soft hyphens narrow their neighbours when measured joined (`getJoinedNarrowing()`), which sums only the pairs that narrow. Chrome returns from some of those: three of the Chromium profile's four lost cases are Inter lines whose hyphen overflows by 0.03-0.56px, as `Tab`, a tab and `sep\u00ADa\u00ADrat\u00ADed` in pre-wrap 16px Inter at 62.5px, where `separat-` is 62.53px wide and Chrome ends the line with `sepa-`. They passed before only because `-` measured 1.6-2px too wide. The fourth is a letter-spaced line of decomposed French in 16px Inter, 0.18px from the width, and the Gecko profile's one is bold 13.5px Apple Symbols, whose synthesized bold Canvas measures 0.5px a word wider than Firefox lays it out. The facts set's `ab A\u00ADVAV` in 16px Arial at 37.34px and 60.49px, 2 Chrome cases, accepted, fail by this test and pass in the prototype that puts the joined width in the line (the entry on words shaped whole, above).
  - A hyphen wider than the syllable after it. Where the first soft hyphen on a line doesn't fit its hyphen and the syllable after it fits without one, the Chromium and Gecko profiles keep the next soft hyphen, whose hyphen overflows, where Chrome and Firefox break the word elsewhere (#323's cases; RESEARCH.md, Dead Ends, Invisible Characters, Controls And Soft Hyphens). Few syllables are narrower than `-`, a third of an em; a Chinese font's U+2010 is a full em. `foo trans\u00ADat\u00ADlantic` in 16px Songti SC at 46.75px is `foo`, `tran`, `sat-`, `lantic` in Chrome and, where a second span starts at the first soft hyphen, `foo`, `trans` with no hyphen painted, `at-`, `lantic`; the profile's second line is `trans\u00ADat-`, 62.59px wide, in both. The one text node failed before #396 too. The spans passed at 46.75-49.25px, with a `-` 11px narrower than the hyphen Chrome paints there, and fail since: 51 of 20,955 fresh cases of that text as one node and as spans in five font lists at 30-125px, all in Songti SC, against 1,054 gained, and in 115 more that pass, the line Chrome ends before the span without a hyphen is reported 16px too wide, where it was 5px too wide (Chrome 154.0.8037.57, 2026-10-01; Firefox and webkit-host lost none of them and gained 2,126 and 2,331).
  - In Chrome and Firefox a hyphen line in Marker Felt or Chalkboard SE, and in Firefox Chalkduster, is 0.5-1.6px wider than its text and either hyphen measure, as before #396; not traced.
  - Firefox's hyphen line is one letter-spacing gap narrower. RESEARCH.md, Measurement Model, has how Canvas can tell a font's own hyphen. #321's side finding 9 (by 2026-09-12).
- Under the Gecko profile, soft hyphens that each have a word joiner or a combining mark after them, under letter spacing, take more lines in a wider box where Firefox doesn't. In 16px Arial in pre-wrap at letter spacing 1, `a`, sixteen times a soft hyphen and a word joiner, U+0301, `b` is 5 lines at 7.3px and 18 at 7.4px in the profile, which ends a line at every soft hyphen once a hyphen and two spacings fit, 7.33px; Firefox has 4 lines at both. Firefox's own count falls where a hyphen and one spacing start to fit, from 18 lines at 6.318px to 4 at 6.349px, and the profile has 5 at both. `a`, eight times a soft hyphen and U+0301, `b` is 4 lines at 7.3px and 10 at 7.4px in the profile, 4 at both in Firefox. A sweep of widths on the stand-in Canvas found the rise in 5 of 3,000 plain cases, with three texts (RESEARCH.md, When A Wider Box Needs More Lines): the first of these, the second with seven soft hyphens, and at letter spacing -4 `a`, ZWSP, U+0308, a soft hyphen, U+093E, `b`, the same four again, `c`, a word joiner, `def`. Firefox's recordings of those paragraphs, at 8 to 12 widths each, never rise with the width, and the profile's failures on them, all under 24px, are accepted as narrower than real layouts. Firefox's rule isn't traced (`bun harness explain`, Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-06).
- Chrome's `text-spacing-trim` (`src/han-kerning.ts`) isn't modeled inside a unit that takes emergency breaks: Chrome fits `」。b` at 33px in 16px Arial, where Pretext charges 32px for `」。` (2026-09-14), and lays out `『是的。』` before a line feed in pre-wrap 16px PingFang SC in 4 lines at 16-31px, Pretext 5; widths miss even where lines match (`中」`, U+00AD, `中` at 24-32px: 24px in Chrome, 41.68px in Pretext; 2026-09-27). Pretext's port takes the premise that each character of a pair takes the font Canvas draws it in, where Blink types both by the font of the one it halts (trims to half width) (no line changed in 5,736 Chinese and Japanese corpus cases, Chrome 153, 2026-09-23). A pair that two rich-inline items split halts as in one node, each mark by its own item's font, as Blink types it (#425; RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, CJK At An Item's Edge), and misses in the same units: where a line fills one that an item boundary splits, a closing mark keeps the halt of its pair at a line end, where Chrome halts it only if it doesn't fit otherwise (items `中中」`, `。中中` at 32px on a stand-in font report 24px for `中」`, the plain-text walker 32px; the lines are the same).

### Negative letter spacing and hanging spaces

Main before #340 matched most of these only while it started the next line with the space.
- At −6 in pre-wrap the browsers don't fit a word after a hanging space, and Pretext does (` A B` in 16px Arial; 7 cases; 3 / 0 / 1; 2026-09-15).
- Chrome ends a line before a space after a soft hyphen, where Pretext hangs the space (`a`, U+00AD, space, `b`; 161 cases; 50 / 2 / 25; 2026-09-15).
- At −1 in pre-wrap, Safari and Firefox give the space after `لا` in `a لا ب` (1px, 32px Arial) a line of its own, where Pretext hangs it (2026-09-15, Safari 26.5.2).
- In pre-wrap Chrome hangs preserved spaces and tabs after an overflowing letter, where Pretext starts the next line with them (by 2026-09-12).
- Where a line's last word fits only with the negative gap after it, for |letter spacing| px from that width, the walker ends the line before the collapsible space or ZWSP after the word, as it fits them against the line's width without that gap (`walkPreparedComplexLines`). The browsers keep the space on the line whatever its width (Blink's `HandleTrailingSpaces`, `line_breaker.cc:2426-2533`, Chromium 153; WebKit's `trimmableTrailingWidth`, `InlineLineBuilder.cpp:1462`; Gecko's `TrimTrailingWhiteSpace`, `nsTextFrame.cpp:11540`). The next line starts after the space, so lines still match, but a flat line's `end` sits on the space and its text drops it, and a soft hyphen right before the space breaks wrongly: in 16px Arial the Gecko profile gives `zz ` / `ab ` / `cd` for `zz ab­ cd` where Firefox gives `zz ab` / `cd` (per shape and font, 10-12 widths at −0.08, 20 at −0.2 and 100 at −1; Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-09-29), and the WebKit profile paints `ab-`. At spacing as negative as a space is wide, the line ends at a space although the next word fits (80 offline catalog layouts, such as `中文 word` at 9.5px). Counting the gap in that fit, one expression, fixes these on the stand-in Canvas but moves about 140 flat layouts per profile, some where main matches Chrome, such as the pre-wrap entry above; it wants a structural form judged by the gate. It is the premise of the next entry.
- Where a run between breaks overflows the line and negative advances after it bring the text's width back under the line's, rich inline takes an item that fits whole onto its line, as Blink takes a text item whole where its shaped width fits (`ShapingLineBreaker::ShapeLine`, `shaping_line_breaker.cc:281-297`, Chromium 153), and the plain-text walker ends the line at that run, as WebKit does, which fits each run between breaks in turn (`TextOnlySimpleLineBuilder.cpp:318`, `InlineLineBuilder.cpp:1585`). With every plain case in white-space: normal predicted as one rich item (2026-09-29), the two disagree on 9 Chrome, 8 Firefox and 25 webkit-host cases, all `catalog/main` shapes under 24px at −4 and −6px letter spacing, such as `A B` in 16px Arial at −6px and 7.8px: the browser takes the item's side in 8, 6 and 5 of them, the walker's in 1, 2 and 17, and neither in 3 webkit-host cases. No real-usage draw differs. Where the browser takes the walker's side in Chrome and Firefox, a combining mark follows a ZWSP or soft hyphen (`a​́b` at −4px and 7px in Chrome). Turning the item's whole fit off in the WebKit profile moves no recorded rich case, but walks every item there, which would slow the rich line functions in Safari (not timed), so rich inline keeps the fit in every profile, and a paragraph of one item takes it before the text walkers (`onlyItem`). Since the one-paragraph design the fit is the whole paragraph's (`findWholeLine()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`): one that fits is one line, without a walk. A paragraph of several items one of which takes less than no room is walked instead (`narrowsLine()`), and there a segment of negative advance doesn't bring a line that overflows back, as no engine lets content make a line narrower: `A` and an item of a word joiner at −1px letter spacing in 16px Arial are one line at 9.68px in all three browsers and two at 9.648px, and the paragraph gives two at both, since the text walkers fit the joiner's no advance before the negative gap after it (one accepted case in each browser, which main passes; the 9.648px twin passes, where the fit alone failed it; 2026-10-05).
- Where a paragraph's whole line ends in preserved spaces, or in tabs where the profile hangs them, and is over the width given by less than the fit allowance, rich inline reports it at its own width and the text line functions at the width given: the whole fit takes a line that fits by the allowance as it is (`fitsWhole()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`), where the walker clamps what hangs to the width with no allowance (`walkPreparedComplexLines()`). One pre-wrap item of `ab` and two spaces, 22.6px wide in the Blink profile on the stand-in Canvas, is 22.6px wide at 22.597px, and its text 22.597px in `layoutWithLines()`. The lines are the same and the two widths less than the allowance apart, in every profile, at any size (2026-10-09).
- Pretext disagrees with itself: text laid out again at its widest line can break earlier, since a space, a ZWSP and a soft hyphen fit before the negative gap the reported width includes (stand-in Canvas, every profile: `--`, ZWSP, space, `ab` in pre-wrap at −1 takes two lines at 30px, the wider about 14px, and at that width the space takes a third line of its own; 2026-09-30). Shrink-wrap callers such as the bubbles demo meet this.

### White space and controls

- The scans read Pretext's collapsed white space, not each engine's (Blink keeps FF as text in normal white space, WebKit CR and FF in both modes); the Gecko scan reads the source's, so its breaks follow Firefox but its widths come from the collapsed text (2026-09-23), but for a CR or FF, which takes no room there since #399, as a lone CR takes none in the WebKit profile since #455 (both below).
- Lone CR, FF and VT in pre-wrap: Pretext takes CR and FF as hard breaks and VT as text, and each engine differs (in Firefox `ab cd`, CR, U+00AD, CR, `ef gh` takes a line too many at 12-48px; the accepted lists' "Pasted C0 and C1 controls"; the README tells apps to normalize `\r`). Two per-engine CR prototypes each lost results main before #340 got right (RESEARCH.md, Dead Ends): trace the engines' line builders first (by 2026-09-12).
- A CR or FF in normal white space takes no room in the Gecko profile since #399, as in Firefox, which takes neither as white space and gives neither an advance (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Firefox, under CR and FF); before, it was a space there, as it still is in the other profiles. The analysis takes it out of the text; keeping it as a control of no advance, as the profile keeps the other controls Firefox hides, was tried and not taken (RESEARCH.md, Dead Ends, Invisible Characters, Controls And Soft Hyphens). What that leaves in Firefox, on a probe of 13,380 cases recorded in Firefox 156.0.1 and not checked in, of which Pretext fails 897 where it failed 1,365 before (lone CR and FF between words and beside white space, soft hyphens and marks, sentences with CRLF and lone CR line ends, rich items; 2026-10-01):
  - White space that touches both sides of one is two runs in Firefox, a space each, and one space in Pretext, whose normal white space keeps one space of two that touch once the CR between them is gone: `see`, space, CR, space, `this` in 16px Arial is 59.60px wide in Firefox and 55.15px in Pretext, and so is `see`, space, CR, LF, `this` (77 cases of 10 texts at 24px and wider, failing before too). With a soft hyphen or bidi control between the two, Pretext keeps both. Where the word after doesn't fit, Firefox also gives the second space a line that shows nothing (`see`, space, CR, LRM, space, `this` at 28-30px).
  - Under letter spacing Firefox gives the CR or FF the spacing, and Pretext, which takes it out of the text, none: `see`, CR, `this` at 1px letter spacing is 58.70px wide in Firefox, eight gaps, and 57.70px in Pretext (9 catalog cases on Firefox's accepted list: 4 of a CR between CJK characters at -1px, whose own gap lets the character before it fit; 2 of `a`, CR or FF, `b` at 1px; and 3 of `a`, CR or FF, `بِبِ((tail` at 1px and -1px, where the Arabic letters take no spacing (Letter spacing), so the gap of the CR or FF is the whole difference, 62.33px against Firefox's 63.33px at 1px; and 5 probe cases that passed before, when its space made the line wider than Firefox's; 22 that failed pass).
  - Rich inline read a CR or FF at an item's edge as the item's white space until the one-paragraph design, whose analysis takes it out as a text's does: on the stand-in Canvas items `see` and CR, `this word` now have no space between them, and `see` and CR, LRM, space, `this word` one, as Firefox has (2026-10-05; the 16 probe cases of two texts weren't recorded again). Of 2,262 rich cases 171 failed, 183 before #399. A probe of 1,128 layouts of a CR at item edges, recorded since, passes in Firefox 156.0.1 (2026-10-07; the lone CR's entry below has its shapes and what it leaves).
  - A space before a CR or FF takes room where the line would end after them: Firefox's fit trims only the run of spaces that ends the line, and the CR, no space to its text run, ends that run (`gfxTextRun::BreakAndMeasureText`, `gfxTextRun.cpp:1152-1160`), though the line's end then trims both (`IsTrimmableSpace`, `nsTextFrame.cpp:921-942`). Pretext, with the CR gone, hangs the space: `see this`, space, CR takes 2 lines in Firefox at 56-59px and 1 in Pretext (24 probe cases of 5 texts, failing before too), and `aaaa A`, space, CR, `V bbbb cc` keeps `A` off the first line in Firefox at 52-55.15px (2 catalog cases on Firefox's accepted list, failing before too). Under 24px Firefox ends a line after a CR where the word after it doesn't fit, so white space around it can sit on a line that shows nothing (85 of 352 cases fail, 89 before).
  - A combining mark right after a CR or FF goes with the character before the CR, and a break Firefox has before the CR is lost (3,429 of the 689,037 CR and FF in a million random strings; no probe case).
  - Firefox draws a control character other than CR as a hexbox outside release and beta builds (`layout.css.control-characters.visible`), so there FF takes a hexbox's width.
- A lone CR in normal white space, which no draw of the real-usage sample holds, takes no room in the WebKit profile since #455, as in Safari, which keeps it in its text item with no advance, no letter spacing and no break but ICU's, after it, which its break scan takes only in text with a character above U+00FF (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Safari (WebKit), under CR and FF, has where); before, it was a space there with no break beside it, as an FF still is. The analysis takes it out of the text, as #399 does in the Gecko profile, and keeps the breaks the scan found around it; keeping it as zero-width glue was built and not taken (RESEARCH.md, Dead Ends, Invisible Characters, Controls And Soft Hyphens). The sample holds no CR in a CRLF either, in any of its 11,901 draws, so none of this moves its headline. What the change leaves in Safari is counted on two probes in webkit-host (WebKit 22625.1.29.11.27, 2026-10-06) that aren't checked in, layouts of texts built to hold a lone CR. The first is 60,192 layouts of texts without an FF (a CR between words, beside white space and at a text's ends, sentences with CRLF, lone CR and LF line ends, random strings, and a CR beside the letters of ten scripts), 47,268 of them in Arial, Times New Roman, Georgia, Hiragino Sans, Thonburi and Courier New, where at 24px and wider lines are wrong on 335 of 44,880 layouts against main's 4,136, 203 of them a wrong line count against 1,869. The second is 36,022 layouts of other texts, recorded with the harness's recorder in Arial, Helvetica Neue, Verdana, Georgia, Times New Roman, Hiragino Sans and Inter served as a web font, some at 1px and -0.5px letter spacing or under keep-all, where at 24px and wider lines are wrong on 1,499 of 32,986 layouts against main's 8,897, 942 of them a wrong line count against 4,425. There, 101 layouts whose lines main had are wrong now, each one right on main only with a line 0.8 to 12px off in width (the kerning, the combining mark and the vertical tab below, and 5 layouts of Devanagari at 1px letter spacing, which both builds measure 2 to 3.5px narrow), and under 24px 23 of 3,036 are, against 375 fixed, 11 of them with main's widths right too (the mark and the punctuation below). Neither count holds the fixed-pitch fonts, which the change gives up (the first bullet); the bullets after it are where main differs from Safari too:
  - Accepted, by decision (RESEARCH.md, Decisions Log, 2026-10-06): the fonts on WebKit's fixed-pitch shortcut. Where the first installed family of a font list is Menlo, Monaco, Courier, Andale Mono, PT Mono or the generic `monospace`, which draws Menlo (the Engine Facts entry has the rule and the measurements), Safari gives a CR a space's width where the text has no letter spacing and is on simplified measuring, and no break. For one CR between two characters that aren't white space, that is what the profile had before by another road, the CR being a space no line ended at: 16px Menlo `ab`, CR, `cd ef` is 77.06px wide in Safari and on main, and 67.43px now, so there a line with a CR is a character narrow and a paragraph can come out a line short. On the first probe's six texts in Menlo, Monaco and Courier at 13, 16 and 20px, at 24px and wider, main has wrong lines on 897 of 6,426 layouts and every line right to 0.05px on 3,935; this has wrong lines on 2,450 and a line a space narrow on the rest. On the second probe's texts with a CR between two such characters, in 14px Menlo, 13px Monaco and 16px Courier at 24px and wider, main has wrong lines on 293 of 4,196 layouts, 64 of them a wrong line count, and every line right on 3,903, and this has wrong lines on 2,260, 1,309 of them a wrong line count; Andale Mono and PT Mono read the same, and of 2,568 such layouts of rich items in 14px Menlo, 220 that main had are wrong. Neither build has the rest of the shortcut: each of two CRs in a row, a CR beside white space or at a text's end, and the CR of a CRLF take a space's width there too (`ab`, CRLF, `cd` is 57.80px in Safari and 48.16px in Pretext), so over all the second probe's texts in those three fonts, at 24px and wider, main has wrong lines on 3,535 of 9,069 layouts and this on 5,546. Canvas doesn't show which fonts take the shortcut. Equal advances don't tell it: Courier New has them and WebKit leaves it out by name, and `ui-monospace` and a fixed-pitch web font have them and take no shortcut, so there a CR takes nothing and this is right (15px Courier New: wrong lines on 223 of 3,073 layouts, 974 on main). A font list goes by its first family that is present: the real-usage sample's two fixed-pitch lists, `"SF Mono", ui-monospace, Menlo, Monaco, monospace` and `"Courier New", Courier, monospace`, 467 of its draws, take no shortcut, and `Menlo, Monaco, monospace` takes it. The harness holds the gap as one facts template, that Menlo text (`facts/layout.test.ts:1575`): of its 11 webkit-host cases, the 6 from 34 to 77.03px fail where main passes, 1 with a wrong line count and 5 with a character on another line, and are on the accepted list under this decision; its case at 1px fails on main too. It reopens with a Canvas fact that tells which fonts take the shortcut, or with a report of lone CRs laid out in such a font.
  - White space that touches both sides of a CR is two spaces in Safari and one in Pretext, as in the Gecko profile: `ab`, space, CR, space, `cd ef` is 61.38px wide in 16px Arial and 56.93px in Pretext. A line feed counts, so a blank line of CRLF text and a space before a CRLF are two spaces as well (`ab`, CRLF, CRLF, `cd` is 43.59px against 39.14px): `line one`, CRLF, CRLF, `line two after a blank` has wrong lines on 83 of 591 layouts and `alpha`, space, CR, space, `beta gamma` a wrong line count on 26 of 591, both as on main.
  - White space between a CR and the text's start or end is a space in Safari, where the CR is content that keeps it from the line's edge, and nothing in Pretext, which trims a text's ends, as main does: CR, space, `foo bar` and `foo bar`, space, CR are 54.26px wide in 16px Arial and 49.81px in Pretext, and take a line more than predicted at 9 of 40 and 9 of 34 box widths of 24px and wider, as in the four other fonts tried.
  - A space before a CR takes room where the line ends after the CR, which it can beside a character above U+00FF: in 16px Arial at 28px, `سلام`, space, CR, `عالم` has a first line 27.95px wide in Safari and 23.51px in Pretext, which hangs the space. Per font, of 5,670 layouts of a CR beside the ten scripts' letters, a width is off on 163 to 227, in Arial each time for this, and lines are wrong on 2 to 6, where main's are on 245 to 308.
  - The letters on a CR's two sides kern with the glyph the font gives U+000D in Safari, and with each other in Pretext. Where that glyph is the font's space glyph, they kern as with a space: in 16px Arial `A`, CR, `A` is 19.58px wide in Safari, 21.34px in Pretext and 25.79px on main, and `WAVE`, CR, `TODAY`, CR, `AT`, CR, `YALTA` has wrong lines on 35 of 1,131 layouts where main has 410. Where the font has a glyph of its own for it, as Helvetica, Helvetica Neue, Times and Palatino do, nothing kerns with it: 16px Helvetica `A`, CR, `V` is 21.34px in Safari and 20.16px in Pretext, which kerns the pair. A word before a CR and a space is now measured with that space, whose kerning is the CR's only in the first kind of font: `A`, CR, space, `B` is 24.91px in 16px Arial and in Pretext, 25.79px on main, and in 16px Helvetica 25.79px in Safari and on main, 24.91px now. In Georgia, which kerns nothing with the space, no layout of the eight letter-like texts has a wrong line or a width 0.05px off. Canvas measures a CR as a space whatever the glyph, so it tells neither kind. For this, 25 layouts of the second probe at 24px and wider whose lines main had, each with a line 3.9 to 12px too wide, are wrong.
  - A combining mark right after a CR goes with the character before the CR in Pretext, as in the Gecko profile, where Safari draws it apart: `a`, CR, U+0301, `b cd` is 42.11px wide in 16px Arial, 39.14px in Pretext and 46.55px on main, and has a wrong line count on 2 of 44 layouts at 24px and wider, 3 on main. In the second probe 47 layouts at 24px and wider whose lines main had, with a line 4.2 to 4.5px too wide, are wrong, and at 5px, in a box narrower than a letter, 6 layouts have a line fewer than Safari, which gives the mark a line of its own as main did, with its widths right. A CR before a vertical tab loses the same way: Pretext measures a vertical tab, which Safari draws as `.notdef`, 7.55px narrow in 16px Arial, main's space for the CR made up part of that, and 24 layouts at 24px and wider whose lines main had, with a line 0.8 to 3.1px off, are wrong.
  - Under 24px Safari gives a CR a line of its own where the character before it fills the line, which a CR that is gone can't take: lines are wrong on 809 of 2,388 layouts, 890 on main, and 25 pinned cases the glue build passed stay on webkit-host's accepted list, 22 of them at 1px. A CR can also start a line where the character after it can't: in a box too narrow for a Han character and the `,` or `!` after it, Safari cuts `好`, CR, `,` before the CR, the first character after `好` that may start a line (`firstCharacterBreakRespectingLineStartProhibitions`, `InlineContentBreaker.cpp:139-158`), and Pretext keeps `好,` whole, as Safari does where no CR is between them; main ended the line at its space, so 5 layouts at 10 to 16px that main had, widths too, have fewer lines than Safari.
  - A CR right after U+2028 or U+2029 takes a line of its own in Safari, at every width, and none in Pretext, which takes the CR out. The separator ends its line (`handleSegmentBreak`, `InlineItemsBuilder.cpp:954-962`), and the CR, a text item (`handleNonWhitespace`, `:1012-1038`), is content for the next, where white space alone leads a line and collapses away (`Line::appendText`, `InlineLine.cpp:346-373`). In 16px Arial `ab`, U+2028, CR is 2 lines in Safari at each width tried from 18 to 200px, the second with nothing visible, and 1 in Pretext; so it is with a space, a tab, a second CR or a line feed after the CR (a CRLF, whose CR is no white space to WebKit either), with U+2029, and in the seven other fonts tried (Times New Roman, Helvetica, Georgia, Verdana, Hiragino Sans, Courier New and Menlo, where the CR's line is a character wide, by the fixed-pitch shortcut above), where `ab`, U+2028 and `ab`, U+2028, space are 1 line in both. Text after that white space, in the same text or in the next rich item, starts on the CR's line after a space: `ab`, U+2028, CR, space, `cd` is `ab` and then a line 21.34px wide in Safari, where Pretext's second line is `cd`, 16.90px, and at 18px, where `cd` doesn't fit beside the space, the CR and the space keep a line of their own, 3 lines for Pretext's 2. Main before #455 was a line short in the same layouts, the CR being a collapsed space there. On three probes in webkit-host that aren't checked in, 672 layouts of 150 paragraphs (WebKit 22625.1.29.11.27, 2026-10-07), Pretext is a line short on 283 of the 287 plain layouts in normal white space where CRs follow a separator and nothing but white space follows them; the other 4 are of `ab`, ZWSP, U+2028, CR, space, whose separator the scan leaves inside a text item, with no forced break, as Safari does. Between #455 and #459 the profile laid the separator out as a control where lone CRs and then white space ended the text, or the rich item, after it, each rich item's text being analysed alone then (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Safari (WebKit), under CR and FF). In a rich item before another item, the control let the next item follow on the separator's line: of 72 layouts of 18 such paragraphs, Pretext was a line short of Safari on 62. A rich paragraph, analysed as one text (the last bullet of this entry), keeps that break with #459's rule or without it, as the item's white space doesn't end the text: its line count is Safari's on 54, all but the 18px ones, though none passes: Safari's second line starts with the space after the CR, which Pretext leaves out at the start of the line after the separator. In plain text the control changed less: Safari's Canvas gives U+2028 no width in any of the eight fonts, nor U+2029 in Arial, so it took no room and went to a line of its own only where the last line had no room for a space before it, or under letter spacing. There it stood in for the CR's line, and 36 of the 287 layouts had Safari's line count, 31 of them passing, by two errors that cancelled; they are a line short now, as the rest were. In a paragraph the rule keeps the break of a separator where the white space that ends the paragraph starts a later item or follows lone CRs (`mapSourceLineBreaks()`, `src/analysis.ts`). The harness has three rich templates for it (`harness/sets/rich.ts`). `abc`, U+2028 before an item of one space holds the rule in a box narrower than a letter: without it the webkit-host case at 1px has a line too many, the separator's own, and the four wider ones pass either way, since Safari's Canvas gives the separator no width. With a space before the separator, `abc`, space, U+2028 before an item of one space, it holds the rule at a real width: Safari hangs the space and has one line from 25.80px, and without the rule the separator takes a second line wherever `abc` fits and the space doesn't, from 25.80 to 30.24px, as webkit-host's case at 25.80px shows. At 1px Safari gives the separator a line of its own after that space, 4 lines for Pretext's 3, a gap with an entry of its own (Rich-inline item edges, a U+2028 after a space in a box narrower than the letter before it); without the rule the 1px case had Safari's count by luck, the separator as a control standing where that line is. `first`, U+2028, CR, space before an item of U+2028 and `second line` came with #459: the second separator ends the CR's line, so Safari's lines are Pretext's. With each item analysed alone, 11 of its 12 webkit-host cases were a line short without the rule; in one paragraph all 12 pass with it or without. In pre-wrap Pretext takes the CR as a hard break and is a line over (`ab`, U+2028, CR, space: 2 lines in Safari, 3 in Pretext; the pre-wrap entry above). It reopens with a segment kind that takes no room and no break and still holds a line, or with a report of text with a CR after a separator.
  - An FF is still a collapsed space, where Safari draws `.notdef` (12px in 16px Arial, so `ab`, FF, `cd ef` is 64.48px wide and 56.93px in Pretext), keeps the white space on its two sides and ends no line at it: of 9,888 layouts of texts with an FF at 24px and wider, lines are wrong on 4,055, as on main, and 46 pinned cases with one are on webkit-host's accepted list, 20 at 24px and wider. Branch `webkit-cr-out-ff` keeps it as a control segment, as the profile keeps NEL, measured as Canvas measures U+0001: lines are wrong on 91 of the 9,888 and all 46 cases pass. It wasn't taken with #455 because it isn't free: `A`, FF, tab, `B` at 21.80px, which main passes with the FF as a space that hangs, takes a line more than Safari's 2, for the 0.88px `A` kerns with the FF as with a space; text with an FF leaves the simple line walk; and rich inline, which reads an FF at an item's edge as the item's white space, then counts the box and a space there, 4.45px too wide in 16px Arial where it is 7.55px too narrow now.
  - Rich inline read a CR at an item's edge as the item's white space until the one-paragraph design (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, Rich Inline As One Paragraph), whose analysis takes a lone CR out of the items' joined text as out of a text: items `see`, CR and `this` have no space between them, as in Safari. A break at such an edge is the one WebKit's check between two boxes finds (`getWebKitBreakBetweenItems()`). It reads the next box's text with the two characters before it, and takes the pair of a CR and a character up to U+00FF from its table, which has no break for it, so a line ends after a CR that ends, starts or is an item only where the character right after it is above U+00FF, where in one text node a letter above U+00FF before the CR can bring the break too, before an ASCII letter: 16px Arial spans `бв`, CR and `cd ef` at 28 and 32px are `бв` and `c`, then `d ef`, and their text in one node is `бв`, `cd`, `ef`. On a probe that isn't checked in, 1,128 layouts of 19 shapes in 16px Arial (a CR that ends, starts or is a span, one inside a span, two across an edge, one across an edge from a space, and a CRLF across and beside an edge, among Latin and Cyrillic letters; each with its spans in one font and with the later spans in bold 20px, and the shapes' 9 texts as one text node each; 24 widths from 12 to 400px), webkit-host lays out all 1,128 as predicted, where main at #455 has wrong lines on 138, 72 of them a wrong line count (WebKit 22625.1.29.11.27, 2026-10-07). White space right after a CR that ends its item, or is an item, is the item's that holds it: a CR is no white space to WebKit, so the run starts after it, and a paragraph's source offsets give no space a CR as its source in the WebKit and Gecko profiles, nor an FF in the Gecko profile (`alignToSource()`, `src/analysis.ts`). A space, a tab or the line feed of a CRLF that starts the next item is so measured in that item's font and letter spacing and is its gap, and a fragment that ends with a space after a CR ends after that space in its item's text, in a paragraph of one item too, and one that starts with such a space starts at it, after the CR. A fragment starts with such a space where an item starts with a CR, or an FF in the Gecko profile, and the white space after it stays in the fragment's text and isn't its gap: before a bidi control or a soft hyphen in the Gecko profile, which the analysis joins with the space, and in a padded item before a line or paragraph separator in the WebKit profile. The space as the CR's item's, which rich inline had until this rule, is right in the Blink profile alone, where a CR is white space, its run's first unit. An item `see`, CR in 16px Arial before an item space, `this word` in bold 20px has three lines in a 66px box in Safari and Firefox, where the CR's item's space made `see this` 65.80px wide and gave two (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Safari (WebKit), under CR and FF, has the three browsers' widths and each engine's source). On a second probe that isn't checked in, 4,234 layouts in 16px Arial with `lang` en (12 widths from 12 to 56px, every half pixel from 58 to 124px, and 400px; webkit-host with WebKit 22625.1.29.11.27, Firefox 156.0.1 and Chrome 154.0.8037.98, 2026-10-07), the 1,752 layouts of a CR before white space across an item's edge (a space, a tab or the line feed of a CRLF; the CR or the space as an item of its own; two CRs; the later item in bold 20px, the first one in it, or the later one under 3px letter spacing) are laid out as predicted in all three browsers, every line within 0.05px, where the space as the CR's item's gave webkit-host and Firefox another line count on 62 each, at the 3 to 12 widths a form where a line holds one font's space and not the other's, and 1,394 lines over a pixel off; the first probe's 20 narrow layouts are within 0.05px too, and Firefox 156.0.1 lays out its 1,128 as predicted, where main at #455 has wrong lines on 72. With an FF for the CR, Firefox lays out 1,022 layouts as predicted where 35 had another line count. What it leaves is two things. One is the FF in the other two profiles, where it is white space (the bullet above, and this section's first entry): Safari draws it as `.notdef`, 12px, and Chrome gives it 5.33px, so 334 of those 1,022 layouts have wrong lines in webkit-host and 181 in Chrome, whichever item the space after it belongs to. The other is a padded item right after such a CR or FF: it is laid out as the same items without the CR or FF are, and so is wrong where they are, in two shapes that only a fuzz holds (two probes that aren't checked in, 51 layouts in Firefox 156.0.1 and 26 in webkit-host, 16px Arial, 2026-10-07; Rich-inline item edges has each shape without the CR or FF, with its widths and line counts). In the Gecko profile an item of only white space and bidi controls leaves its space and no `extraWidth`: `ab`, FF, a 20px item of a space and U+202C with 4px of padding on each side, and `cd` are as wide in Firefox with the FF, with a CR for it or with neither, and Pretext is a line short from 41 to 48px in all three; with the space as the FF's or the CR's item's those two forms gave 47.15px for Firefox's 48.25px, a line short at 48px alone, by luck. In the WebKit profile an item that starts with white space right before a line or paragraph separator counts that space after its start edge: `ab`, CR and an item of a space, U+2028 and `cd` with 4px of padding on each side have a line too many at each width tried under 26px, as they have without the CR; with the space as the CR's item's the form with the CR had Safari's lines at all 13 widths, by luck. On a third probe, 6,588 layouts of 18 other pairs of spans as written, with a CR ending the first span and with an FF there (Han, Hebrew, Arabic, Thai and Hangul words, a soft hyphen or ZWSP before the CR, an LRM, soft hyphen or NBSP after the space, emoji, a padded later span; every pixel from 20 to 140px, and 400px; same builds and day), webkit-host lays the CR form out as the form without it on 2,073 of 2,074 layouts, the one being a CR's own line under 24px (above), and Firefox the CR and FF forms on all 2,074 each, a pair with white space before the CR too aside (the two spaces, above); 39 of webkit-host's CR layouts and 37 of Firefox's CR and of its FF ones that had wrong lines are right, and none that was right is wrong. None of these probes holds word spacing or a right-to-left paragraph.
- Before encoding a rule for controls under pre-wrap, trace Firefox's line-end hang and trim rules; what they do to a CR or FF in normal white space is above. It trims U+1680 at line edges (40 cases; 0 / 2 / 0), fits negative letter spacing before preserved spaces otherwise (13 cases; 1 / 1 / 1), and makes a hidden control a cluster a line must hold (`a`, NUL, `ff` in 24px Amiri; 16 cases; 1 / 11 / 0) (by 2026-09-12).
- Firefox removes a newline between wide characters and, for `ja` and `zh` content, next to East Asian punctuation; content without a language takes the second rule from the OS regional locale, which a page can't read, so there the Gecko profile removes only the first kind (by 2026-09-12).
- Firefox leaves soft hyphens and bidi controls (LRM, RLM, ALM, U+202A-U+202E and U+2066-U+2069) out of its text runs, and since #368 the Gecko profile's analysis gives a run of them to the segment before it (RESEARCH.md, Break Opportunities From Engine Data). Since #399 the white space around them is what the scan's text run keeps, the text taken as one of Firefox's text frames (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Firefox, under text frames). What that still gets wrong, from probes recorded in Firefox 156.0.1 and not checked in: 2,975 fuzzed paragraphs full of controls, of which Pretext fails 304 where it failed 942 before #368 (33aa203b), and 2,790 cases of the shapes below (2026-09-27); and 59,157 cases of white space, soft hyphens and line starts beside controls, in two-word texts, sentences in six fonts, random strings and rich items, 7,979 of them in right-to-left paragraphs, of which it fails 4,287 where it failed 4,617 before #399, and 19,710 more of soft hyphens between white space in sentences, two-word texts, text edges and rich items, 7,030 in right-to-left paragraphs, of which it fails 644 where it failed 1,618 (2026-10-01):
  - The scan takes a text as one text frame, where Firefox ends a white-space run at a control that starts a bidi level run and keeps the white space on both sides of it (RESEARCH.md, Engine Facts, Firefox, under text frames). Which control starts one turns on the paragraph's direction, which Pretext doesn't take. Between white space, RLM or ALM starts one in a left-to-right paragraph unless right-to-left text comes before it, and never in a right-to-left one; LRM starts one in a right-to-left paragraph unless left-to-right text comes before it, and never in a left-to-right one; a PDI after white space inside its isolate starts one in both, except after an isolate against the paragraph's direction between text that runs the isolate's way, as RLI between Hebrew words in a left-to-right paragraph. In 16px Arial `see`, space, RLM, space, `this` is 59.60px wide in a left-to-right paragraph and 55.15px, as in Pretext, in a right-to-left one; `אב`, space, LRM, space, `גד` is 36.63px, as in Pretext, and 41.08px; and `a`, space, RLI, `b`, space, PDI, space, `c` is 39.15px in both, where Pretext's is 34.70px. A line's start splits the same way, the first line's as any other's: Firefox trims the white space each frame starts with while the line holds nothing, so RLM, space, `see this` starts with no space in a left-to-right paragraph and with one, as in Pretext, in a right-to-left one, and LRM, space, `אב גד` the other way around. So does the end of a text: of `see this`, space, RLM, space in a left-to-right paragraph Firefox trims only the last frame's space and takes 2 lines at 56-59px, where Pretext leaves both out (8 cases of two shapes). On a probe of 58 such texts in each direction, 148 of the 4,060 left-to-right cases in 16px Arial fail for it and 68 of the 4,060 right-to-left ones; the facts set pins `ab`, space, LRM or RLM, space, `cd` in both directions (1 Firefox case accepted: RLM in a left-to-right paragraph, at the width where the words join); and NEL, U+0651, `「`, tab, ALM, tab, U+0651, which takes 2 lines at 24px in Firefox and 1 in Pretext and passed before #368, is this, not a mark's base. Where a soft hyphen is in the run with such a control, as in `see`, space, soft hyphen, RLM, space, `this`, Pretext kept both spaces before #399, since its collapse didn't read through soft hyphens: 77 probe cases in 14 texts that passed then fail, and their twins with the mark of the paragraph's direction, which failed, pass. A port that ended runs at level runs under a left-to-right premise wasn't taken (RESEARCH.md, Dead Ends, Invisible Characters, Controls And Soft Hyphens); it reopens with a `direction` option (TODO.md).
  - A text of only such characters keeps one line, where Firefox's block of it has no height, in both white-space modes; as the last pre-wrap paragraph, after a line feed, it adds no line in Firefox or in Pretext (`a`, LF, RLM is one line, 2026-09-30). So the README's sum over paragraphs prepared apart is one line over the whole text's count where the last paragraph holds only such characters, in the Gecko profile only. No segment kind that a line start consumes fits such a text without naming it wrongly, and a text with no segment would leave a rich-inline item of only a control, whose fragment keeps the control painted, with cursors into a text that prepares to nothing; a fix gives rich inline such an item without a line of its own first (Rich-inline item edges). A first pre-wrap chunk of only controls is a line holding them where Firefox's is empty, which moves line text only. In a left-to-right paragraph space, RLM, soft hyphen, space holds one empty line in Firefox, where the same with the soft hyphen first holds none.
  - After NEL or another C0 or C1 control, which stays a segment of its own, a run of controls is a text segment of its own, and the break Firefox takes after the NEL isn't one where a ZWSP follows the run: NEL, PDI, PDF, ZWSP, U+0651, soft hyphen, `a`, LRI, `ab`, space, soft hyphen, `a` in 16px Arial takes 6 lines at 1-4px in Firefox and 5 in Pretext (4 cases, failing before too).
  - An ideographic space that ends a word hangs only where a line takes the word whole, not where it fills the word's graphemes: LRE, `bש`, PDI, U+3000, tab takes 2 lines at 12px in Firefox and 3 in Pretext (5 cases, failing before too).
  - Firefox keeps a white-space run's last space as the base of a combining mark after it, through the bidi controls of its text frame (`IsSpaceCombiningSequenceTail`, `nsTextFrameUtils.cpp:24-30`, `:339-345`), beside the space the run keeps, where Pretext's normal white space keeps one space of two that touch: `see`, two spaces, LRM, U+0301, `x` in 16px Arial is 42.70px wide in Firefox and 38.25px in Pretext, and with RLM, which ends the frame in a left-to-right paragraph, 38.25px in both (7 cases at 1-42px).
  - A soft hyphen before a bidi control offers no break, unless a text frame ends between them: Firefox records a break after the last soft hyphen among what a frame's end drops, and draws its hyphen there (`HasSoftHyphenBefore`, `nsTextFrame.cpp:10514-10537`, from `ReflowText`). In 16px Arial `see`, soft hyphen, RLM, `this` breaks after `see-` at 16-50px in Firefox, in both white-space modes, where Pretext breaks the word between letters, and so does a Hebrew word, soft hyphen, LRM, `this` (115 cases of three shapes; and LF, ALM, soft hyphen, word joiner, `」` at 1-14px, 9 cases of the 2,975, which passed before #368 while the soft hyphen stayed a segment of its own). The scan would resolve levels wherever a soft hyphen comes before a control, under the paragraph's direction (above), and the analysis end the run of dropped characters at that frame's end.
  - Firefox transforms a segment break by the characters around it in its own text frame (`TransformWhiteSpaces`, `nsTextFrameUtils.cpp:104-150`), where `removeSkippableSegmentBreaks()` reads them across frames: `中文`, LF, RLM, `中文` is 68.45px wide in Firefox with the newline's space, and 64px with LRM, and a newline after a Hebrew word and a ZWSP stays a space where one after `ab` and a ZWSP goes (31 cases of three shapes, failing before too; 680 of 400,000 random strings with ZWSP and East Asian characters differ from a per-frame model of `TransformText`). It needs the levels of every Gecko text that has such a newline and a right-to-left character.
  - A line's text keeps a soft hyphen that a bidi control follows in its run of dropped characters, as U+00AD inside a text or space segment (`see`, space, soft hyphen, LRI, `this`), where the Chromium and WebKit profiles, and a soft hyphen that ends a rich item, leave it out. It paints nothing and takes no room, so it matters only to code that reads the string; taking it out of the segment would also take out the break Firefox gives it at a frame's end (above).
  - A soft hyphen stops Firefox's trim of a paragraph's leading spaces as a control does (`nsTextFrame.cpp:10935-10950`), where Pretext consumes the space. A run that starts a paragraph or chunk is text of no width to the walkers, so a space after it takes letter spacing as after a character: LRM, space, `ab cd` at letter spacing 5 fails at 38-42px and 74-78px, and `ab`, LF, LRM, space, `cd ef` in pre-wrap at 9-13px, 37-41px and 70-74px (25 cases, failing before too).
  - The controls are now inside segments that Canvas measures, and Firefox's Canvas, unlike its page, sees them. It gives them letter spacing where a word breaks between graphemes (`ab`, LRM, `cd ef` at letter spacing 3 at 23px: the cluster `b`, LRM takes a gap the page doesn't give it; 1 case, failing before too), a control keeps Canvas from shaping a mark with the letter before it (at letter spacing 2 in 16px Myanmar Sangam MN, a visarga after `က` is 6.90px on the page and after `က`, LRM 15.13px in Canvas; Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-01), and a control between an emoji and its skin tone keeps Canvas from joining them (`ab 👍`, LRM, `🏽 cd`: the page draws one glyph, Canvas two emoji, and the profile, which counts one cluster, corrects the emoji width once, 5px farther from Firefox's width than before; 43 of 90 cases fail, against 33 before).
  - Not traced: `ab`, ZWSP, LRM, `cd ef` at 1-8px (8 cases, which passed before), and RLM, space, PDF, RLM, soft hyphen and two spaces in pre-wrap at letter spacing 2 at 6px (failing before too).
- The Gecko profile makes a soft hyphen right after collapsible white space a zero-width break, and the plain-text walker counts the white space before it in the width of a line that ends after it, where Firefox discards the soft hyphen and hangs the white space: `see \u00ADthis word` at 34.67px in 16px Arial ends its first line at 25.80px in Firefox, which Pretext reports as 30.25px, and so does `see \u00AD` at any width. Rich inline counts it too since it is laid out as one paragraph by this walker; the walk item by item hung it, as Firefox does, by a profile field of its own (Rich-inline item edges has what that moved). A port hangs the space in the walker, for text and rich inline alike. The walker's line start consumes that zero-width break only inside a chunk, so at the start of a text, after a soft hyphen and a space, it holds an empty first line where the word after it doesn't fit, as for `\u00AD \u00ADxyzw` at 16px on the stand-in Canvas (no probe case; 2026-09-27).
- Firefox's line start trims only white space, from where the line starts in a text frame (`GetTrimmableWhitespaceCount`, `nsTextFrame.cpp:967-996`, from `nsTextFrame::ReflowText`, `10935-10950`), so a soft hyphen that starts a paragraph keeps the white space after it on the line, where the line can end: in 16px Arial `\u00AD \u00ADab`, `hyphen` take an empty first line in Firefox at 6-22px, as spans and as one node, and at 80px a 74.75px first line that starts with the space, where ` \u00AD \u00ADab`, `hyphen` take neither and 70.30px. Pretext consumes white space and soft hyphens at a line start alike. The plain-text walker holds a line at the zero-width break the Gecko profile makes of the first text's second soft hyphen, so it gives that text the empty line at narrow widths, and neither text the space, as the second's white space between its soft hyphens collapses into its leading white space since #399; rich lines give neither the empty line, since a rich line start goes on past that break (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries). Where a line breaks at a soft hyphen after other text, the trim stops there too: items `see `, `\u00AD `, `this word` at 1px take an empty line in Firefox after `e` (2026-09-27).

### Rich-inline item edges

Rich inline lays its items out as one paragraph (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, Rich Inline As One Paragraph): one analysis of the items' joined text, each item's segments measured in the item's font, and the text walkers' lines, where from #369 to 2026-10-05 it walked item by item. That closed what this list held from the walk: a rich item that continued a run and started with characters the joined text keeps with the item before it, with the bidi control that starts an item after an item of only a soft hyphen in Chrome (all 51 layouts of `see`, U+00AD, `\u2066this word` at 30-55px and all 81 of `word `, U+00AD, `\u200Fmore text` at 40-80px in 16px Arial pass, where main passes 10 and 18); no hyphen at a soft hyphen that ends an item; the Chromium profile's return from an unfit hyphen only to the break before the item; a soft hyphen right after a chip or a box, in the Blink and Gecko profiles; a bidi control that starts an item after its white space, in Firefox; a chip of only a space; an item of soft hyphens and preserved spaces after spaces that hang; a CR or FF at an item's edge in the Gecko profile and a lone CR in the WebKit profile, which the paragraph's analysis takes out as a text's does (White space and controls has the probe); and the two widths of a one-item pre-wrap paragraph. What's left comes mostly from items measured apart, from white space beside soft hyphens and bidi controls at an item's edge, and from spans that browsers lay out otherwise than their text in one node; a sweep after #460 found eight more shapes, in rare text, that the walk had right by a rule of its own (the list before Other, below). Counts dated 2026-09-27 to 2026-10-04 were taken with the walk item by item, of probes (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries) in Chrome 154, Firefox 156.0.1 and webkit-host. Counts dated 2026-10-05 are of the paragraph: layouts recorded fresh in two document orders in Chrome 154.0.8037.57, Firefox 156.0.1 and webkit-host (WebKit 22625.1.29.11.27), predicted with main at #430 and with the paragraph, and not kept. Counts dated 2026-10-06 were taken the same way in Chrome 154.0.8037.98, with main at #446, which has #425's halts across items and #435's and #446's rules, and with the paragraph on that main.

Measured apart:
- Chrome and Firefox shape and kern across same-font items and Safari doesn't kern there, so item widths miss by about 1px at a boundary, more where one splits a cluster (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, Joined Text). All three join Arabic letters across items, which items measure apart: in 16px Arial items `A`, `V`, `A` are 29.64px wide in Chrome and Firefox and 32.02px in Safari, as Pretext measures them, and items `\u0628`, `\u0628`, `\u0628` are 19.23px in all three, where Pretext charges 34.24px, so items `\u200B\n\u200B \u0628\u0628`, ` \u00AD`, `\u0628`, `\u0628` at 50px take 2 lines where Safari lays out 1 (2 of the 4 webkit-host cases of a 32,830-case probe that lose against main as spans only). Firefox, which drops soft hyphens from its text, also forms an emoji ZWJ sequence across one that starts the next item: items `a👩\u200D`, `\u00AD🚀b` in 12px Arial at 20px take 2 lines in Firefox, the first ending with one astronaut glyph, and 3 in rich inline (1 Firefox case of the 28,435-case probe that main passed). A prepare-time boundary correction for Blink and Gecko was deferred on 2026-09-26, since rich inline with kerning between sibling spans is a large project (RESEARCH.md, The Per-Engine Rebuild And What Counts As Done).
- A soft hyphen that starts an item has no text before it to measure joined with the text after it, as the plain text measures how much narrower Arabic letters join across a soft hyphen, so the line returns from that hyphen where it overflows by less than the joining saves: items `بب ببب`, `\u00AD\u0650ببب بب` in 24px Noto Naskh Arabic at 66px give 3 lines where Firefox and the plain text give 2, and 3 more such probe cases at 66-82px in Noto Naskh Arabic and Arial. Measuring the two items' texts joined would add Canvas calls.

Fullwidth marks and U+3000 at an item's edge (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, CJK At An Item's Edge; in 16px Hiragino Sans). #425 gave the walk item by item Chrome's halt of a pair of marks that an item edge splits, and named six gaps that then showed at more widths, where main's marks, half an em too wide, had made up for them: of 152,572 probe cases, 36 that main passed failed through them, and 14 that main failed with the right line count took a wrong one (Chrome 154.0.8037.57, 2026-10-04). The paragraph has that halt from its analysis. It closes two of the six, a run of U+3000 that ends an item and a chip of only white space, and #446 closed a third, a soft hyphen whose hyphen doesn't fit after a pair an item edge splits (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, CJK At An Item's Edge, has their examples and counts). A closing mark that Chrome halts at a span's end, where the span's text fits its line only so, stays halted where the line goes on, as the walk item by item had it, since an item's own text ended at the mark: the paragraph keeps that by a rule of its own, for an item without `extraWidth` (`itemEndHalts`, `src/line-break.ts`; RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, Rich Inline As One Paragraph, has the rule's counts, and CJK At An Item's Edge has Blink's rule). Each of the others' examples was recorded again with the paragraph and with main at #446 (Chrome 154.0.8037.98, 2026-10-06):
- Left: U+3000 right after a collapsible space, which starts Chrome's next line and which the walker hangs (Line edges). `場所：`, a span `「第三会議室」` and U+3000, `持ち物：`, a space and U+3000 before a bold `筆記用具` fail 14 of the 91 widths from 40px to 400px, on main alike.
- Left: a closing mark that ends a padded item. Blink fits a span's text before the span's end edge. Where the text fits there the mark stays whole, though the edge overflows, and what follows wraps, or the line returns to a break before the span where it has one; where the text doesn't fit, the mark is halted and the line goes on after the span. Rich inline reserves an item's whole `extraWidth` before its text (Boxes, below) and can't tell the end edge from the start edge, so it halts a padded item's mark at a line's end only, where that fits both edges. Two shapes fail:
  - The return to an earlier break: `文字`, a span `（再起動）` with 4px of padding on each side and `して` are `文字` / `（再起動）し` / `て` in Chrome at 116-119px, and `文字（再起動）`, 112px wide, / `して` in rich inline. 8 of the 49 widths from 84px to 132px fail, at 84-87px and 116-119px, on main alike.
  - What follows a halted mark and is narrower than the halt less the end edge: it wraps, where Chrome keeps it on the line. A span `文字」` with 1px of padding on each side before `i` is one line in Chrome at 46-48.75px and `文` / `字」i` at 30-32.75px, where rich inline wraps the `i`, a line too many; with 4px of padding that is 51.81-51.94px. Main passes these, since its walk item by item kept the halt wherever an item's text fit only halted, and so fails where the text fits before the end edge and Chrome leaves the mark whole: the 4px span before `i` fails 8 of 37 layouts on main, at 52-55.5px, where Chrome ends the line after the span and wraps the `i`.
