Over the probes' padded shapes (1px before `i` or a period, 2px before a 1px box or a comma, 3px before a period or around `（再起動）` in 14px Menlo before `l`, and 4px and 8px before `i`), 94 layouts pass on main and fail in the paragraph, 27 by a line too many and 67 by a break, and 124 fail on main and pass in the paragraph (Chrome 154.0.8037.98, 2026-10-06).

  Both close with the end edge's width, which `extraWidth` doesn't split off (TODO.md, the API discussion, has `paddingInline: [start, end]`): the text fits by its start edge, and the halt stays where it doesn't.
- Not checked: a closing mark that ends its item and takes no room on the line, as under a letter spacing of exactly minus its width. Where the content before it overflows the line by less than the mark's halt, the walker takes the mark halted, so the line gets narrower by the halt and what follows may fit after it: `文`, a span `」` at −16px letter spacing and `i` in a 16px font lay out as one line at widths under an em that hold half an em and the `i`, and as `文」`, half an em wide, then `i` at narrower ones down to half an em (2026-10-07, on a stand-in Canvas). No probe holds such a mark: their letter spacings are 1 to 3px, −0.5px and −1px. Chrome's layout of that paragraph at 15.5px would settle it: one line, `文」` then `i`, or `文` then `」i`.
- Left, in fragment widths only: a mark that ends an item is taken for halted where its line is narrower than its segments up to the mark (`createLine()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`), since what follows a halted mark on its line fits in less than the halt. The premise is that what follows takes no less than no room. An item after the mark whose letter spacing is more negative than its letters are wide breaks it: on the unit tests' font, `中中」` and an `i` at −12px letter spacing on a 60px line report fragments of 40px and 5.6px, where the mark's is 48px and the letter's −2.4px (2026-10-06). The line's width and its breaks are the walker's, and right.
- Left, in fragment widths only, in rare text: a mark halted at a line's end where a collapsed space and an item that takes no room follow it on the line, as an item of only a ZWSP, which no item of the real-usage sample is. What a line's width leaves out comes off the line's last fragments and gaps, from its end back (`createLine()`), so the halt comes off that gap and not off the mark's fragment: on the unit tests' font in the Blink profile, `中」` and a space, then an item of a ZWSP, at 3px letter spacing and 37.99px are one line of 38.28px, the mark halted and the 8.28px gap whole, whose fragments report 38px, the mark at its full width, and a gap of 0.28px (2026-10-10). The line's width and its breaks are the walker's.
- Left: a ZWSP that starts a line and holds it empty (Line edges). `、`, a bold `）`, ZWSP and `ー）` at 21px are `、）` / an empty line / `ー` / `）` in Chrome, in one node too, where rich inline and the plain-text walker end the first line after the ZWSP (2026-10-04). The harness's recorder lists no empty line, so its score passes these.

Soft hyphens and white space at item edges:
- A collapsed space before an item of only soft hyphens is the paragraph's space, as in one text: it takes room where content follows it on its line and hangs where the line ends after it (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, Items Of Soft Hyphens And White Space). What doesn't match yet: at the end of the paragraph, where Gecko discards the soft hyphen and hangs the space, Chrome and Safari lay the soft hyphen out after it and give it a line of its own where it doesn't fit, Chrome where the space and the hyphen don't and Safari where the space doesn't (items `see`, ` \u00AD` in 16px Arial take 2 lines in Chrome at 26-35.5px and in Safari at 26-30px, where rich inline and one text node take 1: 20 and 9 of 81 layouts at 20-60px, on main too; 2026-10-05); in the Gecko profile a line that ends at a soft hyphen after a space counts the space, as the text walker does (White space and controls), where Firefox hangs it and the walk item by item did: `see`, ` \u00AD`, `this word` at 32 and 40px, and `see`, ` \u00AD` at 28 and 34px, report a first line of 30.25px where Firefox's is 25.80px, with the right lines (2026-10-05), and where that item is padded the line moves: Firefox places a frame by its end less the trimmable space of the text before it, which a text frame of only discarded characters leaves standing (`CanPlaceFrame`, `nsLineLayout.cpp:1251-1252`; `nsTextFrame.cpp:11421-11429`; read, not stepped through), so `see `, a span of U+00AD with 4px of padding on each side and `-saw` in 16px Arial are `see` with the span / `-saw` at 34-38px in Firefox and three lines in the profile, which keeps the span on that line from 38.25px, where the walk item by item, which gave the span no room, had Firefox's lines at 34 and 35px (the rich set pins it in 16px Courier New with 6px of padding, `rich/item-edges/padded-soft-hyphen-item`, Firefox's case at 50px, accepted; Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-10); the space stays in the width of a line that ends at a zero-width item after the soft hyphen (`see`, ` \u00AD`, `\u200Bab` at 43px: 30.24px, where Chrome's and Firefox's first line is 25.80px) and before a hard break (U+2028 in Firefox); where a line ends at the first of two soft hyphens with white space between them, as in ` \u00AD \u00AD`, Chrome and Safari start the next line with that white space, visible (`ab`, ` \u00AD \u00AD`, `cd ef gh` at 16-100px: 24 of 169 layouts fail in Chrome and 24 in webkit-host, 22 and 22 on main, whose line there was right by its range alone; 2026-10-05), and where the word after it doesn't fit beside it, that white space has a line of its own, which shows nothing and which the paragraph, whose line start consumes it, doesn't have (2 pinned Chrome cases of `ab`, ` \u00AD \u00AD\u200B`, `textword` at 26 and 26.7px: 5 lines in Chrome and 4 in the paragraph, where main's 5 had the ZWSP alone on the second line, a wrong break; the same paragraph at 22 and 22.2px in Firefox, 6 lines for 5; the rule is the text walkers', to land with Chrome's two rules for a soft hyphen beside white space in Line edges; 2026-10-05); and a line separator the WebKit profile makes a hard break takes an empty line more than the browser at narrow widths (items `x`, `yz字 `, ` \u00AD`, U+2028: 22 of 57 layouts at 4-60px in webkit-host, on main too; 2026-10-05). Where Chrome and Safari end the line at the soft hyphen with its hyphen, the line's width now has the hyphen: 35.57px for `see`, ` \u00AD`, `this word` at 36-45px, as theirs.
- In Firefox a line that ends with an item of white space, soft hyphens and a ZWSP after content keeps that item and hangs its white space, where rich lines reserve the white space, so the ZWSP moves to the next line and the space stays in the line's width, or the line ends before the item: items `\u00ADal`, ` \u00AD \u00ADx`, `nation\u00AD`, ` \u00AD\u200B`, ` \u00AD\u200B`, `」`, ` \u00AD\u00A0\u00AD`, ` \u00AD \u200B`, `this word` in 16px Arial at 80px end their first line after the second ` \u00AD\u200B` in Firefox, at 72.95px, and before its ZWSP in rich lines, at 77.40px, where their text in one node passes (8 Firefox cases of an 80,512-case probe that main passed). On the paragraph, of 81 layouts of those items at 60-100px, Firefox and webkit-host fail 9 each that main passes, and Chrome fails 9, 36 on main: at 68.5-72.5px Firefox ends the first line at the hyphen of `nation\u00AD` and starts the second with the visible space of the second ` \u00AD\u200B`, where the paragraph's first line takes the space after `nation\u00AD` and its second starts at `」` (2026-10-05).
- Where the hyphen of a soft hyphen that ends an item doesn't fit, the browser ends the line at an earlier break and rich inline at another: of items `12`, ` \u00AD`, `this `, ` \u00AD `, `\u00A0\u00AD`, `\u0E44\u0E17\u0E22`, `\u00AD `, `\u6F22\u5B57` in 16px Arial at 40-70px, Chrome fails 18 and webkit-host 19 of 61 layouts, 37 and 38 on main, and Firefox none, 10 on main (2026-10-05). A soft hyphen that ends an item paints and charges its hyphen where the line breaks there, as a text's does, and Firefox drops one that doesn't fit, so a line that ends at it there is a hyphen wider than Firefox's. At a padded span's end that width can pass the line's: of items `A hy\u00AD`, a span `phen\u00AD` with 5px of padding on each side, `ated padded span and an`, a chip and `chip` in 16px Arial at 29.3px, the line `en` is 17.80px of text with no hyphen in Firefox, 27.80px with the span's padding, which is what main reports, and 33.13px in the paragraph, on a 29.3px line, with the right lines in both. A reported width that is too wide never clips a bubble sized to it. Of 1,888 Firefox layouts of 32 paragraphs, a line's width is further from Firefox's than main's in 13, 12 of them this paragraph's, and nearer in 56; of 2,049 layouts of a soft hyphen that ends a padded span, at half-pixel widths, Firefox passes 2,013, 1,971 on main, and loses none (2026-10-05).
- Two soft hyphens in a row across an item edge, one that ends an item and one that starts the next: Safari ends the line after the second, so the hyphen it paints is the second item's, where the paragraph ends the line after the first and its next line's start consumes the second, as in one text. Items `extra\u00AD`, a bold ` ordinary`, ` words con\u00AD`, an italic `\u00ADtinue` and ` here` in 16px Arial at 29.3px take 12 lines with the same widths in both, and the line `con` holds both soft hyphens in Safari and one in the paragraph: a wrong break that main passes, 1 of that paragraph's 56 widths and the only one of 1,888 webkit-host layouts of 32 paragraphs that the paragraph loses against main. Chrome fails that width by line count on main too, and Firefox passes it (2026-10-05).
- Safari's line builder for inline boxes charges a soft hyphen's hyphen where a line ends at collapsible white space right after it in the same box, then returns to the line's latest earlier break, as from an unfit hyphen, and paints no hyphen there (webkit-host, 2026-09-26). Items `ab`, ` `, `x\u00AD cd` in 16px Arial end the first line after `ab ` at 35px and after `x\u00AD ` from 36px, where `ab x` and the hyphen take 35.57px, and so do `ab x`, `\u00AD cd` at 34px; a line that ends at white space in the next box, as in `ab x\u00AD`, ` cd`, doesn't charge it, nor does one text node or a single span. Where the soft hyphen starts an item after a collapsed space, as in items `ab\u200B`, ` `, `\u00AD c`, `d` at 26px, the space after it starts the next line, visible. It is that builder's fit of a candidate that ends at a soft hyphen (Returns from an unfit hyphen, below). Of 236 layouts of these four item lists at 18-42px, webkit-host and Chrome fail 105 each and Firefox 17, all of the last list, on main as on the paragraph (2026-10-05).
- A soft hyphen that starts the text right after an atomic item, a chip or a box: all three browsers break there, with the hyphen after the item, where the hyphen fits, and else after the item, and so does every profile: of `ab `, a 20px box or a chip, and `\u00ADcd ef` in 16px Arial at 20-90px all 562 layouts pass in each browser, where Firefox fails 184 on main, and the second line at 20-25.25px is the box's 20px in the Blink profile, where main's is 25.33px with the hyphen (2026-10-05). With an atomic NBSP in the box's place the WebKit profile matched at all but 32px, where the hyphen line is the profile's line-fit tolerance, 1/64px, past the width (2026-10-01). After text in Chrome it's a return from an unfit hyphen (below). A soft hyphen that ends an item right before an atomic item needs room for its hyphen in Chrome and Firefox and none in Safari, which never tests that hyphen (`testsHyphenBeforeAtomic`, `src/measurement.ts`, read in WebKit's source): of `ab cd\u00AD` before a 20px box or a chip, and `x ab\u00AD` before a bold `i`, at 16-110px, Chrome fails 43 of 819 layouts and webkit-host 46, 103 and 64 on main, and Firefox none (2026-10-05).

Firefox's white-space run:
- Firefox collapses white space across soft hyphens and bidi controls in one run that goes on from one text frame to the next (`nsTextFrameUtils::TransformText`). Since #399 the Gecko profile's analysis takes that run from the scan's own text run, in one text and inside a rich item alike (White space and controls has what it leaves), and rich inline follows it across items (RESEARCH.md, Rich Inline Boundaries, Firefox's White-Space Run Across Items): in 16px Arial `ab \u00AD cd` fits on one line at 41px with one space, and an item such as `\u00AD  \u00AD \u00AD \u00AD` holds one. Inside an item the run reads through every control, as in one text, and since #403 across items too (below). Before #399 the analysis kept a space on each side of a soft hyphen, and on the 80,512-case probe 112 of the 220 Firefox cases that 0fef0023 passed and rich lines failed held an item with white space between its soft hyphens, and in 96 of the 220 the line ended at a soft hyphen that ends an item, whose hyphen rich inline leaves out (above); that probe wasn't replayed.
- A gap is one space in one item's font, so it can't hold two: after an item's white space and only bidi controls at another level, as items `see \u200F`, ` this` in 16px Arial, Firefox keeps both spaces and takes 2 lines at 56-59px, where rich lines, like the one node's, give one gap and one line. That's 5 rich set cases in Firefox. On 23,104 targeted rich cases recorded in Firefox, a gap of both spaces fixes 263 and loses 39, where the next item is atomic and its box trims its own space, and on a probe of 41,687 cases dense with bidi controls and right-to-left text it fixes 389 and loses 207 (on the 59,415-case probe, 19 and 5). On the paragraph the space is one segment, in the font of the item it starts in, and still one: 9 of 37 layouts of those two items at 48-66px fail in Firefox, as on main (2026-10-05).
- Firefox ends the run at a character it drops that is at another bidi level than the white space before it, where the text frame splits (`nsBidiPresUtils.cpp:1037-1053`; `ContinueTextRunAcrossFrames`, `nsTextFrame.cpp:2023-2030`), and rich lines, which resolve no levels, carry the run on past every such character, as the Gecko analysis does inside one text. Firefox reads the levels after rule L1 (`unicode-bidi-ffi`, `lib.rs:52-57`). In them a soft hyphen and an embedding or override control always have the level of the character before them (unicode-bidi `lib.rs:1264-1270`, and again in L1, `:1175-1182`), and an isolate initiator resolves as a neutral with the white space before it, so none of these ends the run: items `see \u2066\u00AD` and `see \u2067\u00AD` before an item that starts with a space pass at 21 widths in each direction. White space takes the direction of the strong characters on its two sides where they agree, and its paragraph's, embedding's or isolate's where they don't (UAX #9 N1 and N2, unicode-bidi `implicit.rs:429-465`), and a direction mark is a strong character. So which marks end the run depends on the paragraph's direction, which Pretext doesn't take: U+200F or U+061C after white space that follows left-to-right text ends it in a left-to-right paragraph and not in a right-to-left one, and U+200E after white space that follows right-to-left text ends it in a right-to-left paragraph and not in a left-to-right one. An opening or closing control between the white space and a mark puts them at two levels even where the control has the paragraph's own direction, since an embedding or isolate is a level of its own: items `see \u202A\u200E\u00AD` or `\u202Asee \u202C\u200E\u00AD` before ` this אב more` keep both spaces in a left-to-right paragraph, 129.72px on one line against 125.27px, and one in a right-to-left one, and `see \u2066\u200E\u00AD` keeps both in both. In either direction the run also ends at a mark that follows a mark of the other direction (`PDF \u200E\u061C`) and at a mark against the direction of its embedding or isolate. A PDI ends it after white space inside its isolate where that white space is at another level than the text around the isolate: `ab \u2067גד \u2069\u00AD` in both directions and `ab \u2066see \u2069\u00AD` in a left-to-right paragraph, but not that one in a right-to-left paragraph, nor `אב \u2067גד \u2069\u00AD` in a left-to-right one, which ends it in a right-to-left one, where the port gave one space too. Firefox resolves levels in a right-to-left block and in text with right-to-left characters (Bidi levels, direction and script runs). Rich lines differ only where they carry the run past such a character: before a soft hyphen at the item's end, or in an item of only it and white space; where an item ends right at the character, one gap can't hold both spaces, with levels or without (above). Of items `see`, ` \u200F`, ` this more words here` in 16px Arial at 100px, Firefox ends the first line after `this` in a left-to-right paragraph, 59.60px with both spaces, and after `more` under `direction: rtl`, 96.07px with one, which is what rich lines give in both. From #369 to #403 the Gecko profile read levels for this from a port of Firefox's that took every paragraph as left-to-right, so it was right where rich lines are now wrong and wrong where they are now right. On six shapes with U+200F or U+061C after `see ` or `(q) ` (an item that ends in white space, the mark and soft hyphens, or an item of a space and the mark), each at 31 widths from 60 to 180px, the port passed all 186 left-to-right cases and 173 right-to-left ones, and rich lines pass 173 and 186; the 6 Firefox cases of the rich set that passed from #369 to #403, at 53-123px, are such shapes in left-to-right paragraphs, and the set has none in a right-to-left one. On five shapes inside an embedding or isolate (`\u2067אב \u2069\u00AD`, `\u202Bאב \u200E\u00AD`, `\u202Aab \u200F\u00AD`, `\u2066ab \u2069\u00AD` before text with Hebrew, and an item ` \u2069` after `\u2067אב`) the port passed all 155 cases in each direction and rich lines pass 142. U+200E after `שלום ` got one space from the port and gets one from rich lines, where Firefox keeps two in a right-to-left paragraph: on three such shapes 82 of 93 right-to-left cases pass, and all 93 left-to-right ones. Written as Markdown gives it, a mark or a PDI with a space on each side as the text between a bold and an italic span, in seven paragraphs at 16 widths from 100 to 600px: `Posted by `, `Dana`, ` \u200F `, `01/10/2026` and more words passes 14 widths in a left-to-right paragraph, where the port passed 16, and 16 in a right-to-left one, where it passed 14; ` \u2069 ` after a bold span that opens the isolate, in an English and a Hebrew paragraph, passes 13 in each direction where the port passed 16; the other four, U+200F between Arabic or Hebrew spans, U+200E after a Hebrew name and U+061C before digits, keep their verdicts, 62 of 64 passing in each direction (Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-01; the port's figures are the build before #403's). In real text U+200F stands between two spaces in 32 of 455,648 strings of macOS 27's Arabic, Hebrew and Urdu localizations, text an app sets in right-to-left paragraphs; cut into items at random spaces and laid out on the stand-in Canvas, which has no direction, 7 of 69,547 such paragraphs differ from the port's layout, each with an item of a space and marks.
- An item of only white space and bidi controls, whose run goes on past its controls into its trailing white space, gives the run's one space before it, as the gap of its leading white space (#372). Firefox keeps a run's segment break, or else its first white space (`TransformWhiteSpaces`, `nsTextFrameUtils.cpp:151-193`), so where only the white space after the controls holds a segment break, that space sits after them, and a line that starts at the item keeps it, as Firefox trims a line's leading white space only up to the controls (`nsTextFrame.cpp:10935-10950`): items ` \u2067\n`, `\u200B \u00AD` in 16px Arial at 1px take 2 lines in Firefox, where rich lines take 1, as their line start drops the gap. Of the 14 cases #372 lost on its 7,133-case probe, 8 hold such an item, which #369 passed by giving the run a gap on each side of its controls; in the other 6 that second space made up for widths Pretext doesn't model, such as a space Firefox keeps as a combining mark's base and the space after a soft hyphen that starts a paragraph (White space and controls), 4 not traced (2026-09-28).
- A bidi control that starts an item between two spaces, as in items `see `, `\u200E this word` or `see `, `\u200E`, ` this word`: an open gap, in rare text. None of the real-usage sample's 241 rich-inline draws holds a bidi control. The rich set holds the shape (`rich/item-edges/bidi-control-between-spaces`): `aa see `, then U+200E between two spaces before `this`, with the control in the item of the second space, alone, with that space ending its item, and in pre-wrap, where 8 Firefox cases are accepted, 5 at 48-50px where the profile's line goes back and 3 at 28px where the control and its space hold a line alone (2026-10-10). The control ends the white-space run, so Firefox keeps both spaces (`see`, the two spaces and `this` are 59.60px wide in 16px Arial, 55.15px as one text node), and its scan gives the two one break, after the second (`nsLineBreaker.cpp:318-330`). Firefox still ends a line between them, by how it fits a text frame. It fits the control's frame from the first character its text run keeps, the space (`BreakAndMeasureText`, `gfxTextRun.cpp:1085-1109, 1175-1188`), and where the word after the space doesn't fit, the frame's piece on the line, the part of the frame that the line takes, is its controls with that space, trimmed, or in pre-wrap hung, to no width (`nsTextFrame.cpp:11202-11229`), and a piece of no width fits wherever it starts (`CanPlaceFrame`, `nsLineLayout.cpp:1264-1271`); on a line that has no break yet the frame is cut before the space, as a word too long for its line is cut (`gfxTextRun.cpp:1068-1074`), and its piece is the controls alone (`nsTextFrame.cpp:11162-11164`). So the line keeps its last word, the next starts at the word after the two spaces, and neither holds the control's space: `see `, `\u200E this word` at 26-34px is `see`, 25.80px wide, `this`, `wor`, `d`, and `aa see `, `\u200E this word` at 49-64px is `aa see`, `this`, `word`, from where `aa see`, 48.05px, fits. Where the space ends its frame, the frame has no break to end its piece at, the piece keeps its width, and a line with an earlier break goes back to it (`nsLineLayout.cpp:1323-1334`; `nsBlockFrame.cpp:5361-5379`): `aa`, `see`, `this`, `word` up to 52px. A frame isn't always an item's text. The space ends its frame where it ends its item (`aa see `, `\u200E `, `this word`; a control-only item before an item of a space; a chip, a box or a second control-only item right after the space), and inside an item where the bidi level changes right after it, since Firefox makes a text frame of each level run (Bidi levels, direction and script runs): in a left-to-right paragraph after U+202B or U+2067 before Latin text (`aa see `, `\u202B this word` is four lines up to 52px), after U+200E, U+202A or U+2066 before Hebrew text and after U+200F, U+202B or U+2067 before digits; in a right-to-left one after U+200F, U+202B or U+2067 before Latin text and after U+202A or U+2066 before Hebrew text. The line also goes back where the control's item, or the item that starts with its space, is padded, as a padded span's piece is never empty (`aa see `, `\u200E this word` with 4px of padding on each side: up to 60px), and where a CR follows the control's space, which the fit doesn't trim before a CR (White space and controls): up to 56px. In a left-to-right paragraph with no right-to-left character Firefox resolves no levels, so there an unpadded item's text is one frame whatever its controls: after `\u200E`, a space and `\u202A` the word stays. It stays too where a soft hyphen stands between the white space and the control (`aa see \u00AD`, `\u200E this word`, or the soft hyphen in an item of its own) and where two control-only items come before the space. In pre-wrap the space hangs and the line stays, where the space ends its item and at a level change too, unless the space runs into a tab, which Gecko doesn't hang (Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-09).

  The Gecko profile gives such a control no break, as the scan gives none before a space, and starts a text segment at it. What that gets wrong:
  - Since rich inline is one paragraph (#460), a line with an earlier break goes back to it at every such control. That is Firefox's line where the space ends its frame, and a line more than Firefox's where it doesn't: `aa`, `see`, `this`, `word` for `aa see `, `\u200E this word` at 49-52px, and in pre-wrap at every such control whose space doesn't run into a tab.
  - A line with no earlier break ends before the control, and since #460 with the first space in its width, past the box: 30.25px for `see` in a 26px box, where Firefox's line is 25.80px. The control and its space then start the next line, as before #460, where Firefox leaves the control on the line before and trims the space: at 30px that line is `\u200E this`, 29.35px, for Firefox's `this`, 24.90px, and at 26-29px, where `this` doesn't fit beside the space, the two hold a line alone, 5 lines for Firefox's 4.
  - A soft hyphen right after the control keeps its break, so the profile ends the line before the control there, as before #460, where Firefox's line goes back: `aa see `, `\u200E\u00AD this word` is `aa`, `see`, `this`, `word` in Firefox up to 52px and 3 lines in the profile at 49-52px. At 26-29px Firefox gives the control's frame a line that shows nothing, and so does the profile.

  The walk item by item, as 0.0.9, hung the first space and ended the line there at every such control: `see` at 25.80px, with the same line too many after it, and `aa see` kept at 49-52px, which is Firefox's line where the frame goes on past the space and a line fewer than Firefox's where it ends there.

  A break before the control where a character the text run keeps, other than a tab or a hard break, follows the space in the space's own item was built and measured (#463, whose first commit has it), and isn't in the code: it takes an item for a frame, so it trades. The counts are of eight probes that aren't checked in: 44,281 layouts of 434 paragraphs built around a bidi control between two spaces at an item's start, at widths from 20px up, a pixel or two apart (Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-09; 16px Arial, but for 1,594 layouts with a run in another font). Main at #461 has wrong lines on 2,062 of them. With the break 732 of those are right, and 344 that main has right are wrong, 304 of them by their line count: 284 at a level change after the space, 40 at a padded item and 20 at a CR. Main has 8 of the 20 by luck: after a CR that ends the item before the control, Firefox doesn't trim that item's space (White space and controls), so its line goes back with the control or without, and main's only with it (`on top `, CR before `blue sky` is 4 lines in Firefox up to 48px, and 3 on main from 45px). Five of the probes put U+200E, U+202A, U+2066 or U+200F before Latin text in a left-to-right paragraph wherever the line has an earlier break, and their right-to-left paragraphs start the line with the word before the control: 19,977 layouts, with 160 of the 732 and none of the 344. The other three were built for the frame's end, each of the twelve controls before Latin text, Hebrew text and digits in both paragraph directions, with padding and CRs: 24,304 layouts, with the rest. The break is the walk item by item's line end put back: the build before #460 has wrong lines on all 344 and the right line count on all 732. Three other forms, each against main on the same layouts:
  - The break only in a text with no right-to-left character, as Firefox's own test for resolving levels reads it (`HasRTLChars`, `nsBidiUtils.h:107-111`): 434 right that main has wrong and 60 wrong that main has right, the 40 at a padded item and the 20 at a CR, none at a level change. Losing none of those takes the items' padding and the CR at the rule, which the analysis has taken out of the text by then. A right-to-left paragraph of such text still resolves levels (Bidi levels, direction and script runs), so there it stays a premise, which no probe layout breaks. It was measured once, as a variant that isn't checked in, and nobody else has checked it.
  - A zero-width break in place of the control's segment where the break is given, so that the line ends after the control and the next line's start consumes the space, as Firefox's lines do: 1,625 right that main has wrong and 367 wrong that main has right, the 344 and 23 more, 10 of them after a chip wider than its line (`chip`, an item of a space and `\u200E this word` at 20-29px), where Firefox does start a line with the control and keeps its space; the walkers' line start can't tell that control from one Firefox left on the line before.
  - The first space hung where a line ends at a segment with no break before it, which would take the space out of the width with no line moved. It isn't built: that line end is the walkers' (`src/line-break.ts`), where the analysis alone lays Firefox's bidi controls out (RESEARCH.md, Decisions Log, 2026-09-27).

  Each is a trade or a change to the walkers, so the maintainer's call. A port that is right on both sides needs the levels, and so the paragraph's direction (RESEARCH.md, Decisions Log, 2026-10-01); it reopens with a `direction` option (TODO.md) or with a report of real text that has a bidi control at the start of a styled run. Templates would hold both sides in a left-to-right paragraph: `aa see `, `\u200E this`; that with `\u202B` for the control; and `aa see `, `\u200E `, `this`.

Returns from an unfit hyphen:
- The WebKit profile ports the return of WebKit's builder for text alone (`TextOnlySimpleLineBuilder`), in rich inline too. WebKit builds a line with inline boxes or bidi text with another builder (`LineBuilder`), which differs in ways the profile doesn't take (webkit-host, 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01). It tests a candidate that ends at a soft hyphen with its hyphen (`InlineLineBuilder.cpp:1154-1163`), so a syllable whose hyphen doesn't fit moves to the next line even where the rest of the word fits: a bold `x`, then ` abc\u00ADi efgh`, in 16px Arial at 42.75-44.25px ends its first line after `x` in Safari, as `x abc\u00ADi efgh \u05D0\u05D1` does in one text node at 42-43.5px, where the profile keeps `x abci`, as Safari does for `x abc\u00ADi efgh` alone. Plain text with right-to-left characters rarely shows it: of 1,532 fresh cases of soft-hyphenated Arabic, Hebrew, Persian and Latin mixed with them (8 texts, 4 fonts, both paragraph directions, 24-400px) the profile passes 1,527, and passed 983 before #396; four of the five left are that shape and one is a hyphen line 0.02px past the width. It fits the candidate that opens a padded box without the box's cloned end edge, and its rebuild fits a hyphen without the edges of the boxes still open (`rebuildLineForTrailingSoftHyphen`, `InlineLineBuilder.cpp:1860-1887`), so the first syllable of a padded span keeps its hyphen where only the span's end padding overflows: `Die Ve\u00ADrantwortlichkeit f\u00FCr die `, then `Quali\u00ADt\u00E4ts\u00ADsiche\u00ADrung()` in 12px Menlo with 5px of padding, in 14px Arial at 230-234px ends its first line with `Quali-` in Safari, where the profile returns to the space before the span (31 of 2,076 cases of a padded-span probe, which main passed by keeping every unfit hyphen, against 348 gained; giving every hyphen of a padded item that room lost 180 more). Its rebuild also fits a hyphen against the line's width alone (`InlineLineBuilder.cpp:1873`), where the builder for text alone allows 1/64px more (`TextOnlySimpleLineBuilder.cpp:482-487`); the profile allows it everywhere (`lineFitEpsilon`), so on a line with an inline box it keeps a hyphen up to 1/64px past the width where Safari returns, as the one width left of the atomic-NBSP sweep does (Soft hyphens and white space at item edges, above). The paragraph's walker knows its line's first break whatever items the line spans, so items `tr` in bold and `ans\u00ADi\u00ADt\u00ADlantic` in 16px Arial, which the walk item by item started with `transi-` at 41-42.5px where Safari starts with `trans-`, pass at all 161 widths at 30-70px (2026-10-05).
- A line returns from an unfit hyphen to an earlier soft hyphen inside an earlier item, as the text walker does: items `pa\u00ADd\u00AD` with `a\u00ADm` or `e\u00ADa om` in 16px Arial at 10-70px pass 44 layouts more than main in each browser, 366, 400 and 442 of 442 in Chrome, Firefox and webkit-host, and items `\u0E44\u0E17\u0E22`, ` \u00AD \u00AD`, `12`, ` \u00AD \u00AD`, `\n\u00AD`, `text here`, ` \u00AD \u00AD`, `\u00A0\u00AD`, `\u00ADx`, `ab` at 76px end their second line where Chrome ends it, after `text here`, where main kept an overflowing hyphen after U+00A0 (66.70px wide against Chrome's 72.03px and main's 80.92px). Of those ten items at 60-90px Chrome passes 36 of 61, 35 on main, Firefox all, 50 on main, and webkit-host 36, 42 on main, 7 of them lost at 63.5-66.5px, by where the first line ends (2026-10-05).
- Blink's room for the hyphen is per text item. `BreakText` breaks an item again against the width less the item's own hyphen where the hyphen of the soft hyphen it ends at overflows (`line_breaker.cc:1705-1719`, Chromium 153), and where no break inside the item leaves that room, the line goes back over the items before it to the latest break that fits, with no room (`HandleOverflow`, `:4105-4112`). The paragraph has that room per break (`getHyphenRooms()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`): the hyphen of the break's own item where the break is inside an item, and none at the break before an item's first segment. Of 1,015 probe layouts in 16px Arial with a one-letter syllable before the soft hyphen, as `Bitte die Nebenrollen-i\u00ADkes vor der Regie` (the break after `-` inside the soft hyphen's item after a first item of 20, 12 or 10px, or in a bold or Menlo span; that break right before the item, in the same font, bold, 24px or 12px, after ideographs, or as the third item; and inside an earlier item), Chrome passes 954, where main passes 914 and a paragraph that leaves the first item's hyphen at every break 903 (Chrome 154.0.8037.98, 2026-10-06). Main, whose walk item by item left the next item's hyphen room at the break before it, fails 40 of the 374 with the break right before the item, each by keeping a hyphen past the line's end, and the paragraph none: `Bitte die Nebenrollen-` and a span of `i\u00ADkes vor der Regie` end their first line after `Nebenrollen-` at 157.5-159px in Chrome, where `Nebenrollen-i` fits and its hyphen doesn't. Two more probes, of 31,603 layouts, agree: of the 27,543 at 24px and wider Chrome passes 26,922, where main passes 26,555 and the paragraph before the rule 26,732, and of the 4,060 under 24px 2,277, 2,158 and 2,199. Against main that is 368 fixed and 1 lost at 24px and wider, and the 1 is luck: a hyphen line of 34.76px at 34.75px, which Chrome fits within 1/64px and main kept for want of a break with room. What's left: a break inside an earlier item than the soft hyphen's leaves room for that item's hyphen, where Blink asks only that the line fit (`:4138-4158`). It shows wherever the text between that break and the soft hyphen is narrower than a hyphen and the item ends before the soft hyphen, in one font size too: where the soft hyphen starts the next item or is an item alone. `xy cd-i` before a bold `\u00ADkes vor` in 16px Arial is `xy cd-` / `ikes` / `vor` in Chrome at 46.25-47.75px, and `xy` / `cd-i-` / `kes` / `vor` in the paragraph, a line too many. 29 of the second probe's layouts fail so (the soft hyphen starting a bold or a 22px Georgia item, or alone in an item of 24px Georgia or 9px Arial, at 46.25-47.75px and 52-53.75px), where main, which kept the hyphen past the line's end, has the right line count with a wrong break; the paragraph before the rule failed them the same way. One letter in a font several times the next item's is the same gap, but the harness's recorder splits a line of 32px and 6px text in two, so the 57 layouts of that shape score as failures on every build and say nothing. Closing it takes the walker a second latest break, the last before the soft hyphen's item, which needs no room, beside the latest with room inside it. Blink also ends a text item at a tab or a line feed, where the paragraph's items are the caller's. The other 4 that fail, on main too, are `x ` in 10px before 24px `ab-i\u00ADkes vor` at 40.5-42px, where no break fits and Chrome breaks the word between letters.
- Where a line overflows, Blink walks back over its items and breaks an earlier text item again at its width on the line less one pixel (`LineBreaker::HandleOverflow`, `line_breaker.cc:4137-4156` at Chrome 152), else returns to an earlier break, else lays the line out again breaking anywhere, so Chrome doesn't return into a span to a break that only zero-width text follows there: spans `\u2028\u00AD`, `\u2060\u200Dabab` at 18px in 16px Arial end the first line after `a`, where one text node ends it after U+2028. The Chromium profile returns to the line's latest break, which fails 17 such cases of a 22,771-case probe, as main does, and 38 of 49 layouts of those two spans at 8-32px on the paragraph (2026-10-05). Porting the return waits on two more of Blink's rules (RESEARCH.md, Dead Ends, Invisible Characters, Controls And Soft Hyphens): a break inside a HarfBuzz cluster sits at its graphemes' share of the cluster's advance, and a soft hyphen before a break-anywhere end takes its hyphen.

Spans laid out otherwise than one text node:
- Rich inline takes the premise that the browser lays out spans as their text in one text node, which the plain-text walker predicts. On the 43,462-case probe the browsers lay the spans out otherwise in 72 Chrome, 115 Firefox and 28 webkit-host cases that rich inline loses against main (107, 197 and 59 over three probes of 4,858, 22,771 and 43,462 cases), mostly soft hyphens, bidi controls and separators next to white space at a span's edge. In 25, 63 and 19 of them only a line's first or last invisible character moves: a soft hyphen beside white space at a span's edge whose hyphen one node draws at a line end and the spans don't, or the reverse (spans `a\u2069`, ` `, `\u00AD `, ` ab\u00AD\u200B \u00AD` at 34px in Chrome), or a mark or format character there that one node keeps with the character before it. In the rest the lines break elsewhere: a soft hyphen next to white space at a span's edge (Chrome 36, Firefox 50, webkit-host 7; spans `\u0628\u0650\u0628\u0650`, `\u00AD \u062A` at 18px in Chrome, which one node breaks after the first letter), a line or paragraph separator at a span's edge (Chrome 10, webkit-host 2; spans `xyz字a-a`, `\u0301\u0301\u3000`, `\u00ADword`, U+2029 at 40px in Chrome) and bidi controls next to white space (Chrome 1, Firefox 2), which Firefox resolves per text frame. Firefox also breaks after an item of only a soft hyphen before one that starts with LRI, which it doesn't in one node: items `see`, U+00AD, `\u2066this word` in 16px Arial at 33-50.7px (5 Firefox cases of the rich set; main before #373 failed them too). Each needs the engine's own span boundaries, which the premise leaves out. On the paragraph the three Chrome examples above fail as before: 35 of 57 layouts at 20-48px, 26 of 49 at 8-32px and 15 of 49 at 28-52px, the same on main (2026-10-05).
- On three more probes (80,512, 59,415 and 41,687 cases, each beside its one-node twin), rich lines lose against main 27, 32 and 17 Chrome cases, 19, 71 and 66 Firefox ones and 2, 1 and 0 webkit-host ones that fail as spans only, where the browser lays the spans out as their text in one node and the plain prediction of that node passes. The shapes: combining marks, Devanagari vowel signs and conjunct parts, skin-tone modifiers and emoji ZWJ sequences that items split, which Chrome shapes with the text before them and rich inline measures apart (as `\u0301x` after `see`, ` \u00AD \u00AD-` in 17px Georgia at 54px, which Chrome keeps with `-`); Arabic letters joined across items; lines that end at an item of white space, soft hyphens and a ZWSP (above); combining marks after an item's soft hyphens, which Firefox shapes on the character before them or on a space it keeps before them; an empty line Firefox holds where a line starts with white space and soft hyphens before a ZWSP (White space and controls; 8 cases of `\u00AD \u00ADhyphen \u00AD\u200B`, ` \u00AD ok ` in 16px Times New Roman at 27-46px, of which the paragraph passes 40 of 53 layouts at 24-50px in Firefox where main passes none, 2026-10-05); white space on both sides of bidi controls at another level at an item's end (10 Firefox cases, all fixed by a gap of both spaces, above) and a bidi control after an item's leading white space (8); cases narrower than 24px (3, 35 and 38 in Firefox); a tab between an item's soft hyphens, where Safari ends the line at the space before the item and the WebKit profile takes the tab; and, not traced, bidi controls next to newlines, line separators and soft hyphens (5, 20 and 10 in Chrome, 2, 16 and 9 in Firefox) and tabs, no-break spaces and separators between soft hyphens. #368 took 32 of the 58 and 143 of the 148 Firefox cases that failed so on the last two probes against 33aa203b.

Pre-wrap (#173; counts from the rich set's pre-wrap templates and a 2,152-case probe beside one-node twins, Chrome 154, Firefox 156.0.1 and webkit-host, 2026-09-29):
- Chrome and Firefox count a span's tab stops in the paragraph's font and letter spacing (Blink's `FontForTab` under the tab-size ancestor, `inline_node.cc:2395`, `line_breaker.cc:2963`; Gecko's `ComputeTabWidthAppUnits`, `nsTextFrame.cpp:3875-3906`), which rich items don't carry; rich inline counts each item's stops in the item's own font, which is Safari's rule (`FontCascade::tabWidth`): eight of its spaces in the WebKit profile, and in the Blink and Gecko profiles eight of its letter-spaced spaces, with the least a tab advances from that font too (half its space, half its `0`). That minimum is Firefox's: it reads the font of the tab's own text, not the paragraph's (`PropertyProvider::MinTabAdvance` takes the tab's text run, `nsTextFrame.cpp:3539-3544` and `:1931-1937`), so in the Gecko profile only the stops differ, where Chrome reads stops and minimum both from the paragraph's font (`Font::TabWidth` on `FontForTab`, `inline_node.cc:2137-2143`). Of 192 prefixes before a span of a tab and `b` on one line, every tab ends on one of the paragraph's stops in both browsers; half the span's own `0` gives Firefox 156.0.1's stop for all 192 with each of six spans, where half the paragraph's `0` misses 5 with a bold span and 15 with a 24px one in 16px Georgia, and half the paragraph's space gives Chrome 154's for all 192, where half the span's own misses 2 and 8 with those two; and where the span's space is the paragraph's, an 11px Verdana span in 16px Georgia or the reverse, Pretext's width is Firefox's for all 384 (2026-10-01). A tab in a 12px SF Mono code span in 15px Helvetica Neue prose lands on another stop there (7 Chrome and 7 Firefox cases of the rich set; webkit-host passes them). Since #395 that minimum, and in Firefox the tab that doesn't fit, act at the item's stops, so such a paragraph that had the browser's breaks at other widths can lose them, and others gain them: of `run `, a 13px Menlo span of `make`, tab, `build`, and ` then`, tab, `check the output` in 16px Arial at 60-400px in steps of 5 (69 probe inputs, 2026-10-01), 37 fail in Chrome 154, from 25 (21 that passed fail, at 115-180 and 250-330px, and 9 that failed pass), and 46 in Firefox 156.0.1, from 40 (15 and 9); of `small`, tab, a 24px span of `BIG`, tab, `text`, and a tab and `small again`, 13 fail in Firefox, from 16 (3 that passed fail, at 145-155px, and 6 that failed pass), and 6 in Chrome before and after. At 115px Chrome's first line is `run make`, tab, `build `, 110.27px wide; Pretext had it at 101.75px, its tab taking the Menlo stop 3.7px on, and now moves that tab, under half a Menlo space (3.9px) from the stop, to the stop after, past the line. Another face of the paragraph's family is enough where its space differs, and a monospace span can lose more than it gains: of ten shapes at 120-600px in steps of 8 (61 inputs each), `key `, a bold span of `bold`, tab, `value here`, and ` and`, tab, `more words follow` in 16px Georgia fails at 15 widths in Firefox, from 24 (8 that passed fail), and at none in Chrome, from 23: bold Georgia's space puts the item's stops 32.53px apart, where the paragraph's are 30.93px, so the span's tab ends at 97.6px, where Firefox's ends at 92.8px, and the paragraph's own tab after it, starting 4.8px later, takes the stop after Firefox's, at 247.47px against 216.53px, which is all 8; `use `, a 16px Courier New span of `x = 1`, tab, `# set`, and ` for now`, tab, `and later` in 16px Times New Roman fails at 28 in each, from 14 in Chrome and 16 in Firefox (14 and 12 that passed fail); the four shapes whose tabs are all in the paragraph's font, beside bold, link, code or italic spans, pass at every width, from 35 failures in Chrome and 49 in Firefox. A paragraph font would be a new input (TODO.md); it would give Chrome's stops and minimum and Firefox's stops, whose minimum stays the item's.
- A tab counts from where its item's text starts, after half the item's `extraWidth` on every fragment, as `box-decoration-break: clone` paints it; edges that differ by side miss.
- Rich inline reserves an item's whole `extraWidth` before its text, as Firefox fits a padded span's cloned end edge where a pre-wrap line ends inside the span; Chrome and Safari don't everywhere. At a line feed inside a span with 7px padding, Chrome fits `See let a = 1` in 15px Helvetica Neue without the span's end edge (102.02px), Safari and Firefox with it (110.77px and 108.93px; Safari reserves the end edges of spans already open on the line, `LineBuilder::placedClonedDecorationWidth`), Chrome fits a padded span that ends with spaces that hang without its end edge, which trails with the spaces, as does the opening of a padded span right after them, and Safari keeps a padded span that starts with spaces on a line it ends inside (2 Chrome cases of the rich set; and adversarial inputs, 2026-09-29: 11 Chrome ones of `Unbreakable   ` and a 20px-padded `  spaced code` at 181-199px; 8 Chrome ones at 93-107px of a 7px-padded `Unbreakable   ` before a padded line feed, and of `Run `, then 7px-padded `bun test   ` and `   you push`). Safari's case of the rich set, and its padded spans that start with spaces, pass since the WebKit profile fits such a span by its start edge (below; 2026-10-05). In `white-space: normal` too, Chrome fits a line that ends inside a padded span without the span's end edge, alone in its paragraph or between text, where rich inline fits both edges, as Firefox does on every line and webkit-host from the span's second piece on (Other, below, has its first): Blink takes a cloned end edge off the line's available width only under `BoxDecorationBreakCloneLineBreaking` (`UpdateAvailableWidthFromBaseAvailableWidth`, `line_breaker.cc:454-461`), a runtime flag whose status is `test` in Chromium 153 (`runtime_enabled_features.json5:946-947`), so stable Chrome never reserves it. A 7px-padded `git commit --amend --no-edit` in 12px SF Mono alone in its paragraph keeps `git commit --amend --` on its first line at 158.73px, 151.72px of text (4 Chrome cases of the rich set at 144-158.73px, 2026-09-30). Of 73 probe inputs at 60-225px, Chrome fails 19 and Firefox and webkit-host none (2026-10-01): 8 of 15 widths of that span, 4 of 17 of a lone `bun run check now ok` (60, 105, 130 and 135px), 3 of 20 of a lone `alpha-beta-gamma-delta`, 4 of 10 of `Run `, the first span and ` after` (170, 185, 190 and 195px), and none of 11 of the first span without padding. That probe missed Safari's first piece (Other, below): the three lone spans' ranges lie under 60px, and of `Run `, the first span and ` after` at every 5px from 60 to 225px webkit-host fails only 65px (2026-10-04). Where the line takes only a span's opening, a hard break that starts it, or in Chrome and Safari white space that starts it, rich inline fits the edges each engine fits there (`paddedOpeningFit`, `src/measurement.ts`): where a span with 20px padding starts with its line feed, Chrome and Safari fit the line with the span's start edge only (185.99px and 186.95px), Safari with both where the span holds only white space up to the line feed, and Firefox with both (205.95px). Of the rich set's 151 cases of padded code spans in `white-space: normal`, each browser has 62: Firefox and webkit-host pass all of theirs, and Chrome all but those 4 and one within 1/64px of where its lines change. None of webkit-host's 62, which pass in a fresh recording too (2026-10-04), is at a width where a span's first piece fits with its start edge and not with both, so they don't show Safari's fit of that piece (Other, below).
- A padded span that starts with a hard break, with no break before it (UAX #14 LB6), whose padding doesn't fit as the engine fits it there (above): without a break to return to, Chrome ends the line before the span, leaving a line of its padding, and Firefox and Safari before the last grapheme of the text before it, a preserved space too, before that grapheme's span where the grapheme is all of one, or keep the span where that grapheme starts the line; where that text ends with preserved spaces that overflow the line, Safari keeps the ones that fit (`hardBreakItemRetreat`, `src/measurement.ts`). `Unbreakable` and a span of `\nnext line` with 20px padding in 15px Helvetica Neue keep the line feed from 107px in Chrome and Safari and from 127px in Firefox; `Unbreakabl`, a bold `e` and that span move the `e` to the span's line at 86-106px in Firefox and Safari; `Unbreakable   ` and that span keep the line feed from 86px in Chrome, whose line trails the spaces, from 105px in Safari, which leaves them out of the fit, and from 138px in Firefox, and below that Firefox moves the last space to the span's line and Safari the spaces that don't fit. Chrome's line trails such spaces where they overflow or follow text in one span, which its return breaks before them; after spaces that are a span of their own, which fit, it ends the line before the padded span. The spaces overflow where the content before them fits with the start edges of the spans that open among them and the spaces after those edges don't: `Go`, a 6px-padded space and a 6px-padded `\nnext` in 16px Arial keep the line feed on the first line at 27.5-31.5px and from 44px, and give it a line of its own, of the span's padding, at 20-27px and 32-43.5px, where the overflow comes at a span's start or end edge; the Blink profile takes half the padded spaces' `extraWidth` for their start edge and passes 95 of 121 layouts at 20-80px, 90 on main, and 93 of 117 of `Some words`, an 8px-padded span of two spaces, an 8px-padded `\ncode` and ` tail` at 24-140px, 77 on main (2026-10-05). Of the second, 8 that main passes fail, at 60-67px: the two spaces fit and their span's end edge doesn't, and Chrome ends the line inside the spaces and trails the rest, which the profile doesn't model; what else fails there fails on main too, under 40px. On adversarial inputs recorded 2026-09-29: of 303 in three such paragraphs at 30-200px, 64 Chrome, 0 Firefox and 36 webkit-host inputs failed at d5075562 and 37, 0 and 11 fail now; of 808 in seven shapes of the retreat (the grapheme a span of its own, text split across spans, a padded span before, an emoji, the paragraph's end, keep-all Korean, and `white-space: normal`), 288, 44 and 141 then and 210, 2 and 56 now; of 370 in `white-space: normal` where a span starts with U+2028 or U+2029, which the WebKit profile makes a hard break, 18 webkit-host inputs then and none now; of a reviewer's 1,010 in ten shapes of the rules of e39982fb, 203, 63 and 99 at that commit and 154, 6 and 67 now; and of 707 in seven shapes next to them (spaces in a span of their own or one space before the span, a padded tab or tab and line feed after a chip, padded spaces before a line feed after a chip, and padded spaces after a word that ends with spaces), 246, 204 and 109 at e39982fb and 147, 100 and 67 now. None of them fails that passed at d5075562 or e39982fb. What's left is mostly under 72px, where a padded span's text breaks across lines and rich inline charges its whole `extraWidth` on every piece (Other, below), and Chrome's 65 in `white-space: normal`, unchanged since 619e8ead, where U+2028 is no hard break in the Blink profile; at 72px and wider, Safari breaks inside spaces that are a span of their own before the padded span, where rich inline returns to the break before that span (9 webkit-host inputs at 89-104px), and one webkit-host input of two chips before the span fails as at d5075562. Text that ends with a tab before such a span misses in Firefox and Safari, which move the tab to the span's line, as the text frame's last cluster (Gecko) and its last run (WebKit), where the retreat takes only text and preserved spaces (the walker's retreat before the start edge of a hard break's item, `src/line-break.ts`) and keeps the tab with the text (34 Firefox inputs at 73-139px and 12 webkit-host ones at 85-104px, failing since e39982fb). Of a reviewer's 1,313 inputs in thirteen shapes next to c0bf078c's rules at 30-200px, 561, 532 and 328 fail at e39982fb, 314, 195 and 169 at c0bf078c, and 259, 195 and 169 since Chrome's line takes a run of tabs as an item of its own, as Blink does (`afterTextSpaces`, `src/rich-inline.ts`), and keeps a padded span of only white space after any content (below). Spaces after a line feed follow no text either, as Blink ends a text item at a line feed, so a padded item after them is fitted by its start edge: all 534 Chrome layouts of `ab`, a line feed or CRLF and two spaces, in the first item or in one of their own, before a 6px-padded item that starts with a line feed or spaces pass, 516 on main (2026-10-06). That rule leaves three gaps, each on main too. A tab and two spaces before a padded span that starts with spaces: of `ab`, a tab and two spaces before a 6px-padded `  cd` in 16px Arial, Chrome ends the first line after the spaces at 44.5-50px and moves the span whole to the second, where the profile returns to the break after the tab; 14 of 105 layouts at 8-60px fail, 12 of them there and 2 at 8 and 8.5px, and 5 of the 12, at 44.5-46.5px, by their line count, 3 lines for Chrome's 2, where main has the right count with a character on another line; not traced. Hebrew before the spaces: of `אבג`, two spaces and a 6px-padded `\ncd` or `  cd`, 43 of 178 layouts at 4-60px fail, 41 by their line count; not traced. And a premise under the rule: the spaces count as following text wherever text comes before them in their item, where Blink also ends a text item at the end of a bidi run (`InlineItem::SetBidiLevel`, `inline_item.cc:207-236`, Chromium 153), so spaces between right-to-left text and what follows them at another level are a text item of their own, which no profile resolves (Bidi levels, direction and script runs); whether that is what the Hebrew layouts show wasn't checked. A lone CR or FF, which Blink makes a control item too, is a line feed in the analysis and takes a line Chrome doesn't give it (White space and controls): all 356 layouts of those shapes with one fail, on main too. In Firefox two of those probe's shapes fail that the walk item by item passed, neither recorded there before (Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-06). A ZWSP before the spaces that end the text before the padded item: of `ab`, U+200B and two spaces before a 6px-padded `\ncd` or `  cd` in 16px Arial, Firefox keeps the spaces on the first line, where they hang, and moves only the last of them down with a padded line feed that doesn't fit, where the paragraph returns to the break after the ZWSP, so both spaces start the next line, a line too many or a space on another line: 60 of 73 layouts at 9-38.5px and 36 of 105 at 12-29.5px, of which the walk item by item failed none and 26. With a soft hyphen in the ZWSP's place the paragraph ends the first line at the soft hyphen, with its hyphen, where Firefox keeps the spaces there and paints none: it loses 11 of 73 and 11 of 105 layouts at 18-23px, though it passes 62 and 86 of them to the walk's 24 and 54. webkit-host loses 6 of the 73 with the ZWSP and 6 with the soft hyphen, at 13.5-23.5px, each the right line count with a space on another line. Chrome lays the ZWSP shapes out as the paragraph does (all 178 pass). And a line that holds only a tab before a padded span that starts with a line feed: Firefox keeps the span's opening on the tab's line however far its padding overflows, where the paragraph ends the line after the tab and gives the padding a line of its own, a line too many in 27 of 141 layouts of `first line\n  `, a 6px-padded Menlo `  code`, ` tail\n\t` and a 6px-padded `\nmore` at 21-47px, which the walk item by item passed; it is the tab before such a span of the sentence above, with nothing before the tab on its line.
- After a chip, preserved spaces, a tab that hangs and a line feed without padding stay on the chip's line however far it overflows, whatever items the white space spans, as all three browsers lay out a chip wider than the line; Chrome, though, gives a line feed after such spaces a line of its own there, which rich inline doesn't model: Blink's return keeps the white space after the break after the chip on the line and ends the line there (RewindOverflow, `line_breaker.cc:4332-4424`), before the line feed (17 adversarial inputs of `Ping `, a chip and `   \nnext words` at 30-57px; and 43 of 101 of `Ping `, a chip, a 12px-padded `  ` and `\nnext words`, 19 of them at 86-133px, 2026-09-29). Where that white space spans several items, rich inline keeps it and the line feed on the chip's line since #386, a line short of Chrome, where before it moved them to the next line, a wrong break that gave Chrome's line count (12 probe inputs in three shapes at 30-54px, 2026-09-30). Chrome gives a CRLF there a line of its own too, at the same widths as a line feed, and Safari gives the CR of a CRLF a line of its own after a chip wider than the line, right after the chip too, and after text that fills the line, as `Unbreakable   ` in 16px Arial at 92-104px, where rich inline takes CRLF as one break (probe inputs at 20-200px, 2026-09-30: of a 20px-padded chip in bold 16px Arial and `   \r\nnext`, 18 of 46 Chrome and 22 of 46 webkit-host ones fail, at 20-88px and 20-104px; of the chip and `\r\nnext`, 18 webkit-host ones; of that text and `   \r\nnext`, 9). Chrome also moves a span that starts with white space and goes on past it to the next line whole, its white space too, as that return doesn't trail the tag that opens it (`line_breaker.cc:4384-4419`), but keeps that white space where it starts the paragraph's own text, as rich inline does for every item, since an item doesn't say which it is (72 Chrome probe inputs at 20-91px in each of three shapes, 2026-09-30: that chip before a span `   next`, before ` ` and a span `  next`, and before a tab and a span `  next`; the last passed before #386, whose line didn't take white space split across items). Firefox breaks only after a run of spaces and tabs and doesn't hang a tab, so it moves such white space that runs into a tab to the next line with the tab, whatever items it spans, back to the break after the chip, as rich inline does since #386; the 181 probe inputs of `   \t` after that chip pass since #395 ported Firefox's tab stops and its tab that doesn't fit (97 failed before it, 153 before #386). A run of tabs split across items isn't one run to rich inline, which breaks between the items where Firefox, with no break inside the run, returns to the break before the word: `ab cd`, tab, a bold span of tab and `ef gh`, tab, `ij` in 15px Helvetica Neue at 67-99px starts its second line at `cd` in Firefox and at the span in Pretext (33 of 181 probe inputs, failing before too; 2026-09-30). The run also goes on past an item of only soft hyphens in Firefox, where rich inline's ends (29 of 61 probe inputs of a chip, `  `, a span of U+00AD and `\tgo` at 20-104px, failing before #386 too, and 86 of 181 at 20-200px, 2026-09-30). Where a padded span of white space after the chip moves to the next line, rich inline keeps unpadded white space after it on that line, as Safari does, which hangs each white-space item, where Firefox moves it to a line of its own (12 probe inputs of that chip, a 12px-padded space in SF Mono and spaces at 20-23px, which passed before #386, whose line didn't take white space split across items). A padded span that starts with them stays where the engine fits its opening (above), and Chrome keeps one of only white space on its line however far it overflows, after the chip or any other content, as that return keeps white space and the tags of spans that close among it, and no break comes before white space (UAX #14 LB7); else the line ends at the break after the chip. Where `Ping `, a chip, `  ` and a 12px-padded `  ` can't take the padded span once the spaces before it count, Firefox breaks after the chip and Safari between the two white-space items, and rich inline keeps both on the chip's line (10 Firefox and 10 webkit-host inputs at 83-124px). Safari gives no break between a chip and a line feed after it, as its content runs on to the line break (`nextWrapOpportunity`), so there the line returns to the break before the chip, or keeps the line feed where the chip starts the line. On a reviewer's 606 adversarial inputs (chips before padded spans that start with a line feed, spaces or a tab, one that starts the paragraph, and a tab and spaces then a line feed after a chip; 30-200px, 2026-09-29), 193 Chrome, 69 Firefox and 166 webkit-host inputs failed at cbde4699, 161, 200 and 96 at d5075562, which kept every such span on the chip's line, and 116, 53 and 24 fail now, none that passed at either. What's left: `extraWidth` on every piece under 72px, Chrome's line feed or CRLF after spaces and its span that starts with white space, Safari's CR of a CRLF, Firefox's white space after a padded span that moved, Chrome's end edge after spaces that hang, its and Firefox's tab stops in the paragraph's font (above), and Firefox's run of tabs split across items.
- A padded span that starts with spaces, after text: Safari fits the span's start edge after the text and its spaces, which it counts, leaving only the span's own spaces out, as WebKit's hanging width is its last white-space item's (`setHangingContentWidth`, `InlineContentBreaker.cpp:956-958`); Firefox fits both edges after them, and gives no break between the text's spaces and the span's, so a line that can't take the span goes back to the break before the text's last word, or with none ends before the span, whose spaces then hang on a line of their own; Chrome's line trails them (above). The paragraph's line holds the text's spaces and its latest break, which the walk item by item didn't, so the WebKit profile fits such an opening by half its `extraWidth` (`getOpeningFit()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`) and the Gecko profile returns as Firefox does. In pre-wrap, nine shapes with spaces before the span (`ab ` or `x ab ` and a 3px-padded ` cd` in 16px Arial at 10-60px; `Unbreakable   ` and a 20px-padded `  spaced code` or a 12px-padded `  `; `Run `, 7px-padded `bun test   ` and `   you push`, ` it now`; and three sentences with bold or italic spaces before an 8px-padded span) pass 1453 of 1453 layouts in Firefox, 1446 in webkit-host and 1354 in Chrome, where main passes 1162, 1342 and 1318, and none fails that main passes; six shapes with no space before the span (`ab` or `x ab` and that ` cd`, `Unbreakable` and that code span, a sentence, and that ` cd` after a box or a chip) pass 1212 of 1236 in webkit-host, 1127 on main, all in Firefox and 1135 in Chrome, as on main (2026-10-05). What's left: in webkit-host, 7 layouts of the two padded spans at 62-68px, where Safari fits a padded span's first piece by its start edge (Other, below), and 24 after a box or a chip at 12-14.75px; in Chrome, 99 and 101 layouts, all but 36 of them failing on main too (above has Chrome's fits). After an item of only a soft hyphen, `x ab `, U+00AD and that ` cd` at 10-60px: webkit-host passes all 201 layouts, 171 on main; Firefox 161, as on main, since it gives no break after the item of a soft hyphen there; and Chrome 153, as on main: at 30.25-35.5px Chrome ends the first line after `x ab ` with no hyphen, as the Blink profile does since the break before the item that holds the soft hyphen leaves no room for a hyphen (Returns from an unfit hyphen, above; 2026-10-06, 131 before that).
- Firefox, which doesn't hang tabs, keeps a tab on a line whose content before it fits without the letter spacing after its last glyph, which an item before the tab counts in its width, so under letter spacing a tab at an item's edge next to a space can take the next line where one text keeps it (8 of 800 seeded inputs at 1.5px on the stand-in Canvas, not recorded).
- An atomic item lays its text out in normal white space, as the harness's chips, `white-space: nowrap` inline-blocks, do; a chip styled `white-space: pre` keeps spaces rich inline collapses.
- Plain pre-wrap's gaps carry over: the letter spacing Firefox gives a tab that ends a text run (Letter spacing), lone CR and FF (White space and controls), and tab stops that count from the start of a line the caller narrowed.
- Of 500 real-usage pre-wrap paragraphs (the sample's) split into same-font spans, each one that fails as spans fails as one node too (1 in Chrome, 14 in Firefox, 6 in webkit-host). Of 250 catalog ones split so, 6, 3 and 3 fail as spans only and the browsers lay 4, 3 and 3 out otherwise than one node, all combining marks, joiners, soft hyphens and joined Arabic an item edge splits (Measured apart, above).

Boxes:
- Pretext adds a box's width as given, where browsers round an element's width to their layout unit first, so a line within about a unit per box of its edge can break otherwise (RESEARCH.md, Objects Inside A Line, has each browser's unit and the measured example). The README advises whole or quarter pixels, which every grid holds at the usual pixel ratios. A width or padding under half a unit is none in Firefox: a 0.005px box is an empty frame there, and a span with 0.004px of padding one without a width, where the Gecko profile takes any width above 0 as one (`ab `, such a box and ` cd`, or a 0px box and such a span ` cd`, in 16px Arial: 31 of 121 layouts at 10-40px each; the second passed before #405, while the profile kept a 0px box wherever it fell; Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-01). WebKit's and Gecko's rounding could go in their profiles; Blink's needs the device pixel ratio (TODO.md).
- A box of width 0 right after a padded span whose last piece overflows its line stays on that line in Chrome 154.0.8037.57 and webkit-host, where the Blink and WebKit profiles move every atomic item that doesn't fit to the next line: a span `ab` with 20px of padding on each side, a 0px box and `cd` in 16px Arial at 30px are `a`, `b` with the box, `cd`, and rich inline's `a`, `b`, the box with `cd` (2026-09-30; not traced in either source). Firefox keeps it too, as the Gecko profile does: no break comes before an empty frame that follows text (`emptyAtomicAlwaysFits`, `src/measurement.ts`).
- Where Firefox has a box of width 0 inside a line and Pretext's widths put it past the line's end, Firefox keeps the box and the Gecko profile starts the next line with it when text follows (`setEmptyObjectFacts()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`, and the walker's `placesEmptyObject`); before #405 the profile kept such a box whatever the fit, so these passed by luck. Any width the two disagree on at a line's end does it. Two such disagreements are closed. Letter spacing off Firefox's 1/60px grid: the profile counts it in whole app units since #397, and all 7,215 layouts of a sentence with a 0px box after every space at 120-600px in 16px Arial, Georgia and Helvetica Neue pass at −0.08, −0.16, −0.375, +0.12 and −0.1px, where 94 of the 5,772 at the first four failed with the spacing taken as given (Firefox 156.0.1, 2026-10-02). Tab stops under letter spacing, and a tab that doesn't fit, which follow Firefox since #395: all 1,752 layouts of a pre-wrap tab before a 0px box after a chip, two letters or a sentence pass, where 546 failed with the stops as they were, and of 53 random layouts with a tab under negative letter spacing that the reading of the white space had taken from right to wrong, 52 pass (2026-10-02). What's left: A synthetic bold span: 3 of 1,443 layouts of a mixed-style message in 14px Inter, at 220, 288 and 446px, where Firefox's first line is 219.97px with the space and the box and Pretext's 220.67px. Under pre-wrap, a space before the soft hyphen that ends its item hangs in Firefox, and the run of spaces that hang goes on past the zero-width break the Gecko scan makes of that soft hyphen: all 121 layouts of `ab ` with U+00AD, a 0px box and `cd` in 16px Arial at 10-40px pass, where main fails 31, and all 121 with the soft hyphen in a span of its own (2026-10-05). In normal white space a collapsed space before such a soft hyphen ends the line in the profile, which starts the next line with the box, where Firefox, which drops the soft hyphen, hangs the space and keeps the box: `ab`, a space, U+00AD and a space in one node before a 0px box at 10-13.25px and 18-22px (31 of 121 layouts that main passes; the walk item by item hung that space, as its `spaceBeforeSoftHyphenHangs` did), and a chip, a node of a space and U+00AD and a 0px box at 76-78px, where the chip fits and the space after it doesn't (2 of 36 layouts at 10-80px). A tab that ends its text run under negative letter spacing, which Firefox gives the spacing after a run's last character and the profile doesn't (Letter spacing), so the two disagree on whether the tab overflows the line: `a` with a tab, a span of U+00AD and a 0px box in pre-wrap 16px Arial at −3px and 10px are one line in Firefox, 8.6px wide, the tab ending 3px before its stop, and three here, the tab on the second (six such shapes fail at 10px and pass at 5, 14 and 20px; they fail without the box's rule too; 2026-10-02). The last piece of a padded span, which is charged the span's whole padding (below). Against main at #403, of 18,675 layouts of all these shapes in Firefox 156.0.1, 3,418 pass that failed and 41 fail that passed: 39 with that pre-wrap space before a soft hyphen, 1 with a space narrower than nothing at −6px after the box (below), and 1 with such a tab at −2px (2026-10-02).
- The Gecko profile reads the white space before a 0px box past a line's end from the text (`setEmptyObjectFacts()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`), back through any number of items of soft hyphens alone, and the soft hyphen that gives a break before the box too, but asks only where the box sticks out of the line. At letter spacing below minus a space's width a collapsed space is narrower than nothing and can pull the box back inside the line, where Firefox breaks the line after the space whenever the text before it is past the line's end: `ab ` and a 0px box in 16px Arial at −6px letter spacing and 2px are `a`, `b` and the box in Firefox 156.0.1, and `a`, then `b` with the box in rich inline (4 of 2,609 layouts at 0 to −6px). On the paragraph `ab ` and that box at −6px and 1-30px fail 6 of 59 layouts in each browser, 3 on main, all at 1.5-5.5px (2026-10-05). At that spacing Firefox also ends a line that rich inline's widths fit: `a`, a node of a space and U+00AD, a span of U+00AD, a 0px box and `b` at −6px and 5px are the box's line and then `b` in Firefox, and one line in rich inline (1 of 556 layouts, not traced). With soft hyphens among the white space before the box, in normal white space, 68 of 1,152 layouts still fail, each with the box on Firefox's line: Firefox keeps a collapsed space beside a soft hyphen visible at a line's edge, as a soft hyphen stops its trim (White space and controls), and rich inline consumes it (2026-10-01).
- A paragraph that holds a soft hyphen marks every break the scan gives before text for its walk (`prepareRichInline()`, `RETURNABLE`), and one that holds none doesn't. Since #446 a line's return from an unfit hyphen reads no mark, but the marks still decide where the Gecko profile ends a line that an object of width 0 sticks out of, so there a paragraph's lines can differ with a soft hyphen elsewhere in it. Without the marks, 211 of 200,000 generated paragraphs lay out otherwise in the Gecko profile on a stand-in Canvas, every one with a box or an atomic item of width 0, 184 of them only at 60px or narrower, and 1 in the profile for engines Pretext doesn't recognize; none in the Blink or WebKit profile (2026-10-06). Which of the two Firefox lays out wasn't recorded, so the marks stay as the design was checked with them; one rule for both kinds of paragraph wants those shapes recorded in Firefox first.
- Text that starts with U+3000 right after a 0px box past a line's end: the ideographic space hangs in Firefox, so the box stays and the text after the space starts the next line, where the Gecko profile moves the box down with the space, a line too many (`ab `, a 0px box and U+3000 with `cd` in 16px Arial at 18-22px are `ab` with the box, then `cd` in Firefox 156.0.1; 31 of 121 layouts, a line too many before #405 as well, with the space on a line of its own). A text node of only a ZWSP between two 0px boxes there sends the line back in Firefox, to before the first box, where the profile keeps the first box and starts the next line with the ZWSP; under pre-wrap Firefox keeps all three on the line, inside it after the space that hangs, and the profile breaks before the ZWSP (31 of 121 layouts each, before #405 too; 2026-10-01). Neither is traced in the source. The U+3000 case needs rich inline to hang an ideographic space that starts an item after content past the line's end, which it doesn't. Nor does it between two runs: `文字`, U+3000, a 0px box and U+3000 with `です` in 16px Hiragino Sans or PingFang SC keep both runs on the first line in Firefox, the second hanging, where the profile starts the next line with the second (24 of 148 layouts at 16-160px that main passes, in normal white space and pre-wrap, with 8 that main fails passing; 2026-10-05). For the ZWSP node the source read so far predicts the profile's lines, not Firefox's: a text frame of only a ZWSP has no width, so `CanPlaceFrame` places it as an empty frame, and the line breaker leaves no break after a ZWSP that ends a text run (`nsLineBreaker::Reset`), so what sends Firefox's line back is unknown.
- Padding on an item of only soft hyphens takes no room in rich inline where the soft hyphen is a hyphenation break of its own, as between two letters or after a box, where Firefox lays the span out as a frame as wide as its padding: `ab `, a 0px box, a span of U+00AD with 4px of padding on each side and `cd` in 16px Arial at 18-22px are `ab`, the box with the span, and `cd` in Firefox 156.0.1, and two lines in rich inline (60 of 97 layouts at 10-34px, before #405 too; 2026-10-01). Chrome and webkit-host give that span its padding too: 60 of 97 layouts fail in each of the three, on main as on the paragraph (2026-10-05). The padding takes room, since #460, where the scan makes the item's soft hyphen no hyphenation break of its own, a segment of zero-width glue or a zero-width break, which holds the item's `extraWidth` as a letter would: in the Blink and WebKit profiles before a character no break comes before, as a bidi control, a hyphen or a fullwidth comma, and in the Blink profile a quotation mark; in the Gecko profile before a bidi control after text, and after a space or an ideograph, where Firefox draws no hyphen. There the paragraph alone has the browser's lines in 252, 212 and 209 cases of the sweep's set at 24px and wider (Rich-inline item edges, the sweep after #460; Firefox's 212 is read from the recorded height, and is 209 by the harness's rule). On a stand-in Canvas a soft hyphen with 4px of padding on each side adds 8px before `\u200Ebar` after `foo` in every profile, before `-bar` or `，bar` in the Blink and WebKit profiles, before `‘bar` in the Blink profile, and after `foo ` or `中` in the Gecko profile, and nothing between `foo` and `bar`, or before `bar` at the paragraph's start, in any (2026-10-10). Between two letters it takes none: `foo`, that span and `bar` in 16px Arial are three lines at 24-30px in each browser, the span with its padding on the second, and two on main as on the build before #460 (16 Chrome, 15 Firefox and 15 webkit-host layouts of 58 at 24-81px fail on both; Chrome 154.0.8037.98, Firefox 156.0.1, webkit-host, 2026-10-10).
- White space that ends the paragraph after an atomic item of width 0 past a line's end: as a text node of the paragraph's own it gets no frame in Firefox (`nsCSSFrameConstructor.cpp:5278-5286`, firefox-156.0), so the item stays, and in a span it gets one, with a width, which sends the line back to the break before the item (`setEmptyObjectFacts()`, `src/rich-inline.ts`). An item doesn't say which it is, and the Gecko profile takes the text node: `ab `, a 0px box and a span of one space in 16px Arial at 18-22px are `ab` and then the box in Firefox 156.0.1, and one line in rich inline, as are 11 of 1,443 layouts of an eight-word sentence that ends so at 120-600px in three fonts (2026-10-01). Porting the span needs an item to say that it is a span, which no rich item does. A last node of soft hyphens and then white space isn't a node of white space alone, so it gets a frame either way, as wide as its space, and the profile sends the line back for it since #405: `ab `, a 0px box and U+00AD with a space are `ab` and then the box at 18-22px in Firefox, in a text node as in a span.
- White space that hangs comes out of the text widths of a line's last fragments, never a box's width, so where it is wider than the fragments of its own spaces, as under negative letter spacing on an item of a bidi control and spaces, the rest comes out of the text before the box, where an atomic NBSP standing in for the box gives it up itself, and the line can be narrower than its box's fragment: `xy `, an 8px box, `\u200F` and two spaces at -1px letter spacing, and `next` in pre-wrap at 20px give a second line 7px wide whose box fragment is 8px, and at 30px fragments of 19px and 8px where the stand-in's are 20px and 7px, in all three profiles (stand-in Canvas, 2026-09-30; lines and line widths agree; no browser was asked which is right).
- A ZWSP after content that overflows its line: Chrome and Safari start the next line with it, which it holds though nothing follows, where the text walkers, and so the paragraph, hang it on the overflowing line. After a chip wider than the line Firefox hangs it too: `ab `, a chip `wide chip here` and an item of a ZWSP in 16px Arial at 18-110px are three lines in Chrome and webkit-host, the last empty, and two in Firefox, so the paragraph passes all 66 layouts at 10-140px in Firefox, 15 on main, and 15 in Chrome and webkit-host, where main passes all, as the walk item by item gave an item of only a ZWSP a line in every profile.
